Claim 2 "any fixed rule" is restated against label-derived fixed rules, with the 3,000-versus-200 label caveat; claim 2 as worded and claim 3 stay not_supported. L6a and L6b remain the right next steps.

## L6b 2026-10-18 pass

**Hypothesis:** The 10% gain of frozen config B (stratum agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated, k0 50) survives when the frozen table is fitted on only 200 p0 prompts, the same label count as the refit. If so, the L5a gain is a fixed-ranking effect that needs little labelling. Null: a 200-label table gains at or below +2.0 pp on L2b or on L4b.

**What was run:** avrl/exp/l6b_frozen_label_budget.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l5a_frozen_table.py (L5a unchanged), launched by avrl/exp/L6b.sh. CPU only, run directly in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No GPU job. Rollouts are the existing feats files (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct pools, as in E01 and the L2b and L4b entries). Label: proxy_error (majority vote vs gold, GSM8K). Fit pool: p0, 3,000 prompts. For each subset i in 0 to 19, 200 distinct p0 prompts are drawn without replacement with numpy RandomState(100 + i). Config B (36-stratum posterior, k0 50, prior 1 minus agreement) is fitted on those 200 prompts, frozen, and never refit. Eval pools: L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts). Budget 10%, int(0.10 x n_sim) docs per split. Split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds each. Gap is frozen minus plain fixed on the same split and tie seed, paired by seed. A subset mean is the mean of its 8 split gaps.

**Numbers** (hit rate in %, gap in pp, mean over 20 subsets x split seeds 0 to 7; sd is over the 20 subset means, ddof 1):

| pool | fixed 10% (%) | frozen, 200 labels (%) | 200-label gap (pp) | sd over subsets (pp) | subsets above +2.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | refit B floor 40 (pp) | frozen, 3,000 labels (pp) | 200 minus refit B (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 73.24 | +4.72 | 1.71 | 18 of 20 | +1.53 to +6.40 | +5.02 | +5.65 | -0.29 |
| L4b | 77.60 | 82.73 | +5.12 | 1.92 | 20 of 20 | +2.75 to +8.35 | +5.01 | +8.38 | +0.12 |

Of the 160 split-level gaps per pool, 146 are above +2.0 on L2b and 154 on L4b. Refit B references: L2b from runs/L3a/fresh/result.json, L4b from runs/L4b/result.json (floor 40). Per-subset values are in runs/L6b/result.json and table.md.

**Gates (all PASS, checked in result.json and recomputed from it):** plain fixed at 10% reproduces runs/L2b/result.json and runs/L4b/result.json at 0.000000 pp with the same calls per split. The 3,000-prompt table reproduces runs/L5a/result.json (+5.65 on L2b, +8.38 on L4b) at 0.000000 pp. Spend gate: 5,376 checks, 0 failures. Every subset has 200 distinct prompts (row positions match the RandomState draws). Fixed picks are identical across all tables. Zero eval question texts occur in p0. Exit code 0, no traceback in run.log.

**Verdict against the pre-registered rule:** the pass threshold is a mean above +2.0 pp on both pools. L2b (+4.72) and L4b (+5.12) both clear it. The kill condition (at or below +2.0 on either pool) is not triggered. Overall PASS. Both pools are within 0.3 pp of refit B at floor 40, which is the branch the plan named for the label-efficient fixed-ranking reading.

**What it changes:**
- The label confound in L5a is largely removed on these pools. At about 200 labels a frozen ranking keeps +4.72 pp on L2b (refit B +5.02) and +5.12 pp on L4b (refit B +5.01). The 10% gain does not need the 3,000-label table.
- At approximately equal labels, the adaptive refit does not beat a fixed ranking on these two pools. The refit's gain is reproduced by a fixed, label-derived ranking, so nothing here shows that adaptive placement of labels produces the gain. The refit's label count (about 200 in-budget labels) is taken from the L5a critic and was not recounted in this run.
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): not_supported (unchanged). L6b tests the online config B refit against a fixed table, not the learned allocator. The learned arm (greedy_online) is already logged as a kill on G01 and has not been tested at equal labels. Restated: a label-efficient fixed ranking is the supported reading of the 10% gain.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): not_supported (unchanged). The table uses rollout-side strata only (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated), so this run gives no support to a prompt-space source.

**Caveats:**
- The 20 subsets are not 20 independent label draws. Nine of 20 L2b subset means are exactly +6.40, and several L4b values recur, so many 200-label tables probably select identical docs (identical per-split gaps; the selected sets were not compared directly). The "18 of 20" count therefore overstates independent confirmation. The mean is still an average over 20 valid draws.
- Label count still matters on L4b: +5.12 at 200 labels against +8.38 at 3,000, so about 3.3 pp of the L4b headline depends on more labels. On L2b the difference is about 0.9 pp.
- The lowest L4b subset mean is +2.75, 0.75 pp above the bar.
- Pool composition: the L4b test-row versus remaining-train-row question raised by the L5a critic is untouched here. L6a is still open.
- Refit B at floor 40 on L2b comes from runs/L3a/fresh/result.json, as in L5a, because runs/L2b/result.json holds floor 10 only.

**Follow-ups** (backlog ids L7a to L7c, under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 7 (after L6b)"):
1. L7a, learned arm at equal labels (CPU, extends L5c). greedy_online fitted on the same 20 subsets of 200 p0 prompts, compared with the 200-label frozen table at 10% on L2b and L4b. Pass: more than +1.0 pp above the frozen table on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value high: the only equal-label test of claim 2. A kill is the likely result, given the G01 kill, so the value lies in confirming it at matched labels.
2. L7b, label-count curve for the frozen table (CPU, existing feats). Config B at 50, 100, 200, 400, 800, 1,600 and 3,000 labels, 20 subsets each, at 10% on both pools, with the count of distinct selected doc sets per size. Pass: above +2.0 at 50 labels on both pools. Kill: at or below +2.0 at 50 labels on either. Expected information value medium-high: it locates the L4b label dependence (+5.12 to +8.38) and tests whether it comes from strata coverage (92 to 99% of docs in table at 200 labels) or posterior precision, and it gives the effective number of distinct tables behind the 20 subsets.
3. L7c, prompt-space cell in the frozen 200-label table (CPU, existing feats). Add cell30, which is in the p0 and L4b feats files (confirm in L2b first), to the stratum key with agreement kept in every key, and rerun the same 20 subsets. Pass: more than +1.0 pp above the rollout-only 200-label table on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value high for claim 3, since no logged run tests a prompt-space cell in the frozen setting. A null here is weaker than it looks, because 200 labels leave many of the expanded strata empty and they score the prior, so report stratum coverage with it.

Critic note (L6b, 2026-10-18). Checked the L6b entry against runs/L6b/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag, exp/l6b_frozen_label_budget.py (there is no exp/L6b.py; the script is that file, launched by exp/L6b.sh), the L6b section of lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, the reference results (runs/L3a/fresh/result.json, runs/L4b/result.json, runs/L5a/result.json) and the rollouts.parquet, groups.parquet and feats.parquet of p0, L2b and L4b. Independent checks are exp/l6b_critic_indep.py (own table fit, split build and picks; it imports nothing from the experiment modules) with the log in runs/L6b_critic/indep.log.

What checks out. Every number in the entry matches the raw files: L2b +4.72 (sd 1.71, 18 of 20 subsets above +2.0, min +1.53, max +6.40), L4b +5.12 (sd 1.92, 20 of 20, min +2.75, max +8.35), fixed 68.52 and 77.60, frozen 73.24 and 82.73, refit B floor 40 +5.02 and +5.01, 3,000-label +5.65 and +8.38, differences -0.29 (exact -0.294) and +0.12 (exact +0.117), 146 and 154 of 160 split-level gaps above +2.0, nine L2b subset means at exactly +6.40, in-table share 92 to 99% (lowest 91.6% on L4b subset 9). The run is clean: exit code 0, run.log has no warning or traceback, nothing truncated, all gates PASS in result.json. My own replay of all 20 registered subsets (row positions equal the RandomState(100 + i) draws, 200 distinct pids each, tables fitted with plain pandas, splits 0 to 7 x 8 tie seeds at 10%) reproduces all 160 per-split gaps on each pool to 2e-14 pp, with every policy taking exactly int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs (0 failures; calls per split 219, 226, 222, 223, 236, 231, 230, 223 on L2b and 208, 199, 201, 213, 198, 205, 201, 216 on L4b, identical for fixed and frozen). No leakage: agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated, majority and proxy_error rebuilt from rollouts.parquet equal feats.parquet and groups.parquet on all three pools with 0 differences, the majority tie rule is not gold-aware (251, 255, 317 prompts with a tied top count), zero question overlap p0-L2b and p0-L4b, gold reaches only the fit labels and the hit counting. The verdict follows from the pre-registered rule: the mean over 20 subsets x 8 splits is above +2.0 on both pools (L2b +4.72, L4b +5.12), so PASS, and the complementary kill condition is not met.

Corrections (rule verdict unchanged; wording and strength changed).
(1) Non-independence is now measured, not guessed. Comparing the frozen picks directly (splits 0 to 7, tie seed 0), the 20 tables give only 9 distinct selected-set signatures on L2b (9 distinct subset means) and 17 on L4b (16 distinct means), although 19 of the 20 tables rank their top 6 strata differently. So "18 of 20 above +2.0" on L2b rests on at most 9 distinct selections; L4b's "20 of 20" is much closer to 17 independent-looking selections. The 20 label subsets themselves are close to independent (mean pairwise overlap 12.9 prompts against 13.3 expected).
(2) Label-draw noise does not threaten the pass, but single tables can fail. 100 fresh draws (RandomState(1000 + i)): L2b mean +4.90, 89 of 100 tables above +2.0, lowest -0.14; L4b mean +5.61, 98 of 100 above, lowest +0.34. A partition of p0 into 15 disjoint 200-prompt subsets: L2b +4.17 (se 0.57, 12 of 15 above), L4b +5.35 (se 0.63, 13 of 15). The entry's "min +1.53" is the minimum of 20 draws, not the floor: about 1 table in 9 on L2b and 1 in 50 on L4b falls at or below +2.0.
(3) Eval-pool noise, not in the entry, is the larger uncertainty. The se over label draws is about 0.4 pp, but a bucket bootstrap of the mean-over-20-tables gap (200 resamples of 75 cell100 buckets with replacement, 3 tie seeds; duplicated buckets inflate spread) gives L2b +4.85, sd 2.07, 95% interval +1.70 to +9.08, 188 of 200 above +2.0, and L4b +5.09, sd 1.89, interval +1.69 to +8.98, 190 of 200 above. The pass is firm in direction (94 to 95%) but the lower 95% bound sits about 0.3 pp below the bar on both pools, so the 8 overlapping splits do not give a 95% margin.
(4) The L4b composition question is not "untouched" in its consequence, and my stand-in answer is adverse at 200 labels. Using the same stand-in as the L5a critic (pid below 1319 are the test rows, buckets from the cell100 clustering of the sub-frame; my 3,000-label value on test rows, +3.39, reproduces the L5a critic's), the 20 registered 200-label tables give on the L4b test rows (1,319 prompts, 26.2% proxy error) a mean gap of +1.22 (sd 1.90, 8 of 20 tables above +2.0, min -1.55, max +3.78), and on the remaining-train rows (1,473 prompts, 17.0% error) +8.58 (20 of 20, min +3.95, max +12.75; 3,000-label table +12.74). So the pool figure of +5.12 is a blend dominated by the remaining-train rows, and "a frozen ranking keeps +5.12 on L4b" and "the 10% gain does not need the 3,000-label table" are not supported on the test rows, where the 3,000-label table adds about +2.2 pp over the 200-label tables (+3.39 against +1.22). This is a stand-in, not the real L6a run; L6a should evaluate the 200-label tables as well as the 3,000-label table.
(5) "Same label count as the refit" and "label confound largely removed" hold for the fit labels only. The frozen arm's 200 labels are in addition to its 10% eval-time checks; plain fixed and the refit get no such labels (the refit learns from its own checks). Total labelled by the frozen arm is therefore the 10% budget plus 200 fit labels, and 200 labels is 8.4 to 9.1% of an L2b sim pool (2,196 to 2,369 docs) and 9.3 to 10.1% of an L4b sim pool (1,989 to 2,162 docs), so the frozen arm spends close to twice the labels of the refit overall. The refit label count, not recounted in the entry, is now recounted from calls_per_split: L2b 219 to 236 (mean 226.3), L4b 198 to 216 (mean 205.1), so the 200-label table has about 12% fewer labels than the refit on L2b and 2.5% fewer on L4b. What the data support is "a fixed ranking fitted offline on 200 labels matches the online refit's gain (-0.29 L2b, +0.12 L4b, both inside eval-pool noise)", not equal total-label cost; claim 2 at equal total budget is untested.
(6) Minor: cell30 is present in the L2b feats file, so the L7c "confirm in L2b first" condition is met. The pre-registered backlog text for L6b says "Between is inconclusive", but with pass above +2.0 and kill at or below +2.0 there is no band between; the ledger correctly applied only pass or kill.

Corrected verdict: PASS by the rule, unchanged (200-label frozen minus fixed at 10%: L2b +4.72, L4b +5.12, both above +2.0, reproduced to 2e-14 pp). Carry it as: a frozen config B table fitted once on 200 p0 labels gains +4.7 pp on L2b and +5.1 pp on L4b over the full pools, level with refit B (+5.02, +5.01), robust to the label draw in mean (fresh draws +4.90 and +5.61) and firm in direction against eval-pool resampling (188 and 190 of 200 above +2.0, 95% lower bounds about +1.7), but with these limits: the L2b result rests on 9 distinct selections, individual tables fail the bar about 1 time in 9 on L2b, the L4b pool figure is carried by remaining-train rows (stand-in test rows +1.22 at 200 labels, below the bar), and the frozen arm uses 200 labels beyond the 10% budget. Claim 2 stays not_supported as worded; claim 3 stays not_supported.

## L6a 2026-10-18 split: 3,000-label table PASS, 200-label tables KILL (registered test rows)

**Hypothesis:** The 10% gain of frozen config B (stratum agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated, k0 50) holds on the registered GSM8K test rows of the L4b pool (pids 0 to 1318, 1,319 prompts, 26.2% proxy error), for the 3,000-label table and for the 200-label tables of L6b. Null for a table: mean test-row gap at or below +2.0 pp.

**What was run:** avrl/exp/l6a_test_rows.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l6b_frozen_label_budget.py (L6b unchanged), launched by avrl/exp/L6a.sh. Changes: the L4b eval frame is cut to pid <= 1318 before the cell100 split (so the MIN 3 filter and bucket shuffle run on the test rows), the reproduction gate is the L5a critic stand-in on the test rows, and the context draws reuse the same job. CPU only, run directly in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I, no GPU job, about 5 s. The 3,000-label table is fitted once on all p0 prompts. The 200-label tables are the 20 L6b subsets (numpy RandomState(100 + i), 200 distinct prompts each), each fitted, frozen and never refit. Budget 10%, int(0.10 x n_sim) docs per split, split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds each, gap paired by tie seed against plain fixed.

**Gates (all pass):** plain fixed on the test rows gives 84.503% and a 3,000-label gap of +3.392 pp, against the L5a critic stand-in (84.50%, +3.39), max abs diff 0.003 pp (tol 0.05). Spend: 11,008 checks, 0 failures. Every subset has 200 distinct prompts. Fixed picks identical across tables. Test question text found in p0: 0 rows.

**Numbers** (hit rate in %, gap in pp, frozen minus plain fixed at 10%):

| table | mean gap (pp) | sd (pp) | above +2.0 | fixed 10% | frozen 10% | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3,000-label, test rows, mean over split seeds 0 to 7 | +3.39 | 2.21 | 6 of 8 split gaps | 84.50 | 87.90 | PASS |
| 200-label, test rows, mean over 20 subset means | +1.22 | 1.90 (over subset means) | 8 of 20 subsets | 84.50 | 85.73 | KILL |

Per split, 3,000-label table: +4.90, +3.62, -0.39, +1.39, +2.78, +5.93, +2.97, +5.94. Subset means run from -1.55 to +3.78, with 12 of 20 at or below +2.0.

Context only, outside the verdict (3,000-label table): the 1,473 remaining-train rows (pid 1319 and up, 17.0% error) give +12.74 pp (sd 2.02, 8 of 8 split gaps above +2.0). 64 random 1,319-prompt halves of the full L4b pool give a mean of +7.60 pp (sd 3.26, 64 of 64 above +2.0, min +2.16, max +17.18). The first six halves reproduce the L5a critic's halves exactly (+2.79, +8.83, +7.30, +8.93, +6.79, +6.08).

**Verdict:** The 3,000-label table passes on the registered test rows, so the L5a L4b PASS survives the composition question at test-row scale, but with a thin margin (+1.4 pp over the bar, 6 of 8 splits above). The 200-label recipe fails there: its mean over 20 label draws is +1.22 pp, below the bar, and only 8 of 20 tables clear +2.0. The L6b pool figure on L4b (+5.12 pp) is carried by the remaining-train rows and does not carry to the registered test rows. Refit B on the same test rows was +0.58 pp in the L4b critic (runs/L4b_critic/subcuts.log, stand-in), so the 3,000-label frozen table still beats the refit there, but the 200-label claim does not hold at test-row scale.

**Limits:** The 200-label mean is over 20 label draws on one fixed eval set (its label-draw standard error is about 0.42 pp, and the eval-side bucket bootstrap was not run on the test rows). The 3,000-label result is one fit with 8 overlapping split seeds. Buckets are the cell100 stand-in, not the split-column cut used by the fit and sim, as in the L4b and L5a critics. The test-row numbers are a re-run of the same stand-in, so they confirm the stand-in and do not test a different split.

**Next:** Any claim that config B's gain needs only about 200 labels must now carry the test-row figure (+1.22 pp, KILL) beside the full-pool figure (+5.12 pp). Claim 2 stays not_supported as worded. Files: runs/L6a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag.

## L6a 2026-10-18 kill (200-label tables), pass (3,000-label table, registered test rows)

**Hypothesis:** The 10% gain of frozen config B over plain fixed holds on the registered test rows of the L4b pool (pids 0 to 1318, 1,319 prompts, 26.2% proxy error), for the 3,000-label table and for the 200-label tables of L6b. Null for a table: mean test-row gap at or below +2.0 pp.

**What was run:** avrl/exp/l6a_test_rows.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l6b_frozen_label_budget.py (L6b unchanged), launched by avrl/exp/L6a.sh. The only change is a pid filter on the L4b eval frame (--eval_pid_max 1318) before the cell100 split. CPU only, run directly with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I, no GPU job, no refit. Rollouts are the existing feats files: fit pool p0 (3,000 prompts), eval pool L4b (2,792 prompts, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts as in L6b). Label: proxy_error (majority vote vs gold). Config B: 36 strata (agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated), k0 50, prior 1 minus agreement. Fitted once on all 3,000 p0 prompts, or on 20 subsets of 200 p0 prompts (numpy RandomState(100 + i)), each frozen and never refit. Budget 10%, int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs per split. Split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds each, gap paired by tie seed against plain fixed. Buckets: the cell100 column of runs/L4b/feats.parquet (stand-in, as in the L4b and L5a critics). Test question overlap with p0: 0 rows.

**Gates (all PASS):** plain fixed at 10% on the 1,319 test rows gives 84.503% and a 3,000-label gap of +3.392 pp, against the L5a critic stand-in (84.50%, +3.39), max abs diff 0.003 pp (tol 0.05). Spend gate: 11,008 checks, 0 failures. Every subset has 200 distinct prompts. Fixed picks are identical across tables.

**Numbers** (hit rate in %, gap in pp on the 1,319 test rows; gaps are frozen minus plain fixed at 10%):

| table | fixed 10% (%) | frozen 10% (%) | gap (pp) | sd (pp) | above +2.0 | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3,000-label, mean over split seeds 0 to 7 | 84.50 | 87.90 | +3.39 | 2.21 | 6 of 8 splits | PASS |
| 200-label, mean of 20 subset means (each the mean of 8 split gaps) | 84.50 | 85.73 | +1.22 | 1.90 (over subset means) | 8 of 20 subsets (min -1.55, max +3.78) | KILL |
| refit B floor 40, test rows (baseline, stand-in from runs/L4b_critic/subcuts.log, not rerun) | n/a | n/a | +0.58 | n/a | n/a | n/a |
| context: 1,473 remaining-train rows (17.0% error), 3,000-label table | 69.35 | 82.09 | +12.74 | 2.02 | 8 of 8 splits | context only |
| context: 64 random 1,319-prompt halves of L4b, 3,000-label table | n/a | n/a | +7.60 | 3.26 | 64 of 64 halves (min +2.16, max +17.18) | context only |

Per split, 3,000-label table: +4.90, +3.62, -0.39, +1.39, +2.78, +5.93, +2.97, +5.94. Subset means, 200-label tables: +3.46, +1.62, +3.46, +0.34, +3.33, +3.76, +3.78, +2.40, -0.73, +1.58, -1.55, +2.76, +2.11, -0.95, +0.34, -0.16, -1.55, +0.29, +1.71, -1.55.

**Verdict against the pre-registered rule:** The 3,000-label table has a mean test-row gap of +3.39 pp, above the +2.0 pass threshold: PASS. The 200-label tables have a mean of 20 subset means of +1.22 pp, at or below +2.0: KILL. The two rules are complementary, so there is no inconclusive band. The L6b 200-label PASS does not carry to the registered test rows. Note that the refit B baseline is only a stand-in (+0.58 pp, one cut).

**What it changes:**
- The 3,000-label frozen table still clears the bar on the registered test rows (+3.39 pp, 6 of 8 splits above). The L4b headline of +8.38 pp is inflated by the remaining-train rows (+12.74 pp there). On the test rows alone the L4b figure is +3.39.
- The L6b recipe claim (a table fitted on about 200 labels keeps the 10% gain) is killed at test-row scale: +1.22 pp, 8 of 20 tables above the bar. Any claim that config B needs only about 200 labels must carry +1.22 pp on test rows beside the +5.12 pp full-pool figure.
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported as worded. The 3,000-label table that passes is a fixed, label-fitted ranking, not a learned allocator, and it was compared only with plain fixed. The learned arm is still untested at equal labels (L7a).
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. The table uses rollout-side strata only, and L6a tests whether the gain transfers to other rows, not what causes it.
- Cut-level spread: the registered test rows sit at the low end of random 1,319-prompt halves of L4b (+3.39 against a half mean of +7.60, sd 3.26, about 1.3 sd below). The test-row figure is therefore a low draw of the cut and should not be read as the typical value without L8a.

**Caveats:**
- The 200-label mean is over 20 label draws on one fixed eval set. The eval-side bucket bootstrap was not run on the test rows.
- The 3,000-label result is one fit with 8 overlapping split seeds.
- Buckets are the cell100 stand-in, not the split-column cut used by the fit and sim, as in the L4b and L5a critics.
- The run report's numbers were recomputed from result.json (split gaps, subset means, sd with ddof 1, counts, gates) and match. The run.log and table.md agree with result.json.
- A first L6a section was already appended by the run itself (header "split: 3,000-label table PASS, 200-label tables KILL"). This entry repeats it in the requested shape. One of the two should be removed when the log is next tidied.

**Follow-ups** (backlog ids L8a to L8c, under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 8 (after L6a)"):
1. L8a, 200-label tables on the 64 random 1,319-prompt halves of L4b (CPU, existing feats). Pass: mean gap over the halves above +2.0 pp. Kill: at or below +2.0 pp. Expected information value high: it decides whether the 200-label KILL is a property of the recipe or of the registered cut.
2. L8b, label-count curve on the registered test rows (CPU, existing feats). L7b with the eval restricted to pid 0 to 1318, at 50, 100, 200, 400, 800, 1,600 and 3,000 labels, 20 subsets each. Pass: mean gap above +2.0 pp at 1,600 labels. Kill: at or below +2.0 pp at 1,600 labels, so only the full 3,000-label table clears the bar on test rows. Expected information value high: it sets the label cost the thesis can claim on held-out rows. If L7b runs first, run this version rather than the full-pool one.
3. L8c, stratum-structure null on the test rows (CPU, existing feats). The 36 strata keep their sizes, but their labels are permuted across the p0 prompts before the 3,000-label fit, then the table is frozen and evaluated as in L6a. Pass: true table minus permuted table above +2.0 pp (mean over split seeds 0 to 7). Kill: at or below +2.0 pp. Expected information value medium for claim 3: it tests whether the gain depends on which prompts share a stratum, and no logged run has a size-matched null.


Critic note (L6a, 2026-10-18). Checked both L6a sections against runs/L6a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag, exp/l6a_test_rows.py, exp/L6a.sh, the shared helpers in exp/l1b_tuned_threshold.py, runs/L5a_critic/indep.log and composition.log, runs/L4b_critic/subcuts.log, and the p0 and L4b feats.parquet. Independent checks are exp/l6a_critic_indep.py (own code, no import of l1b_tuned_threshold or l6a_test_rows), log in runs/L6a_critic/indep.log.

What checks out. Every number in the entry matches the raw files: the 3,000-label split gaps (+4.90, +3.62, -0.39, +1.39, +2.78, +5.93, +2.97, +5.94), mean +3.39, sd 2.21 (ddof 1), 6 of 8 above +2.0, fixed 84.50 and frozen 87.90; the 20 subset means (min -1.55, max +3.78, 12 of 20 at or below +2.0, 8 above), mean +1.22, sd 1.90, frozen 85.73; the remaining-train rows (+12.74, sd 2.02, 69.35 to 82.09, 8 of 8); the 64 halves (mean +7.60, sd 3.26, 64 of 64, min +2.16, max +17.18, first six equal the L5a critic's); the refit B stand-in +0.58 (subcuts.log, cell100, floor 40, B 10%, splits 0 to 7); the spend count 11,008 (86 pools x 128). My own reimplementation (frozen table rebuilt in pandas, sim split, budgets 95 to 101 checks per split, tie seeds 0 to 7) reproduces the 3,000-label split gaps to 6e-15 and all 20 subset means to 1e-15, and gives PASS and KILL. The verdict follows from the registered rule: +3.39 is above +2.0 (PASS), +1.22 is at or below +2.0 (KILL), no inconclusive band. No leakage: the frozen score of an eval doc is a lookup on (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated) only; picks are identical when gold, majority and proxy_error are dropped from the eval frame; the fit labels come from p0 only; question overlap with p0 is 0 for the 1,319 test rows, the 1,473 remaining rows and all of L4b (the entry gates only the test rows). Eval-time budget is equal for the two policies (both take int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs, n_sim 957 to 1,017). The run is clean: exit code 0, run.log has no warning or traceback, nothing truncated.

Corrections and gaps in the entry.
(1) The "survives the composition question" wording for the 3,000-label table is stronger than the data. Eval-side noise was not run on the test rows (the entry says so); I ran it. Bucket bootstrap of the 100 test cell100 buckets, 97 of them with at least 3 docs (200 resamples with replacement, splits 0 to 7, 3 tie seeds): 3,000-label gap mean +2.94, sd 3.09, 95% interval -3.64 to +8.39, 130 of 200 above +2.0 (65%). 64 fresh bucket-shuffle cuts of the unmodified test rows (seeds 100 to 163, 8 tie seeds): mean +3.12, sd 2.26, min -1.00, max +9.88, 41 of 64 above +2.0. So the PASS is a mean over the bar by 1.4 pp with about two in three resamples above it, not a result established at 95%. The 200-label KILL is firmer: bootstrap mean +0.97, sd 1.65, 49 of 200 above +2.0; fresh cuts mean +1.04, 14 of 64 above, min -1.83, max +4.51; label-draw se 0.42. 17 of the 20 subset means are distinct (three tables share -1.55, two share +3.46, two share +0.34), so the 20 draws contain fewer distinct selections than draws.
(2) "The registered test rows sit at the low end of random 1,319-prompt halves ... a low draw of the cut (about 1.3 sd below)" misreads the halves. A random half is a mixture of about 47% test rows (range 45.1% to 49.4% over the 64 halves) and 53% remaining-train rows, so its gap is a blend of +3.39 and +12.74 (naive blend +8.35, observed +7.60). The test rows are a different population (26.2% proxy error against 17.0%), not a random cut, and the z-score against the halves has no meaning. The same applies to L8a as written: a 200-label pass on the halves would mostly reflect the remaining-train share and would not say whether the registered-test-row KILL is recipe or cut. The registered test-row figure stands as the answer to the registered question.
(3) Budget accounting is equal at eval time only. The frozen arm also uses 3,000 (or 200) pre-paid fit labels against 95 to 101 eval-time checks per split for both arms; plain fixed uses no fit labels. "The 3,000-label frozen table still beats the refit there (+3.39 against +0.58)" compares a table with about 30 times the eval-time label count against a refit that learns from its own roughly 100 checks, so it does not show the table beats the refit at equal labels. Claim 2 at equal total labels stays untested.
(4) The L5a follow-up that registered L6a (backlog text above) also asked for 64 random 1,319-prompt halves of L2b. The run did the L4b halves only, and the entry does not say the L2b halves were dropped. They are outside the verdict.
(5) Housekeeping: there are two L6a sections in this log with the same numbers (the run's own and the loop's). The verdict lines agree.

Corrected verdict: unchanged by the registered rule. 3,000-label table on the L4b test rows: PASS, +3.39 pp (6 of 8 splits above +2.0), but thin: 65% of bucket resamples and 41 of 64 fresh cuts above +2.0, so carry it as "above the bar by 1.4 pp, not established at 95%", at 3,000 pre-paid labels. 200-label tables on the L4b test rows: KILL, +1.22 pp (8 of 20 tables above +2.0; resampling mean +0.97, 49 of 200 above), so the L6b PASS (+5.12 pp full pool) does not carry to the registered test rows. Claim 2 and claim 3 stay not_supported.

## L7a 2026-10-18 kill (both pools)

**Hypothesis:** At equal labels, the learned online allocator beats the best label-fitted fixed rule at the same eval budget. The learned arm is greedy_prior (the G01 greedy_online branch: K = 1, logistic scorer on the six cheap features, refit every R = max(budget // 8, 40) verified labels), initialised from the same 200 p0 prompts that fit the 200-label frozen config B table. Gap = greedy_prior minus the frozen 200-label table at 10%. Pass: the gap is above +1.0 pp on both L2b and L4b. Null: at or below +1.0 pp on at least one pool.

**What was run:** avrl/exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l6b_frozen_label_budget.py (L6b unchanged), launched per pool by avrl/exp/L7a.sh, then merged by avrl/exp/l7a_combine.py. CPU only, run directly in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No GPU job, no gpu_run.sh. Same 20 subsets as L6b: numpy RandomState(100 + i) draws 200 distinct p0 prompts. Split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) docs per split. The frozen arm is L6b config B, fitted on the subset's 200 labels and never refit. The greedy arm starts from a StandardScaler plus LogisticRegression(class_weight balanced) scorer fitted on the same 200 p0 labels. Each step takes the top-scored unseen doc. After every R verified eval labels, the scorer is refit on the 200 p0 labels plus all verified eval labels so far.

**Assumption (stated as the plan requires):** G01 greedy_online has no p0 fit step and starts from random scores, so the 200-label initialisation is new code. The refit includes the p0 labels, the variant most favourable to the learned arm, so a KILL is the strong reading. Two smaller choices: refits use max_iter 1000 (G01 uses 500), and score ties are broken by the per-seed random draw (G01 uses argsort). Neither matters here: the spread of greedy hits across tie seeds is 0 on every split of both pools, so the eight tie seeds give identical picks and the 20 subsets are the only replicates.

**Numbers** (hit rate in %, gap in pp, mean over 20 subsets x split seeds 0 to 7; sd is over the 20 subset means, ddof 1):

| pool | plain fixed 10% (%) | greedy_prior (%) | frozen 200-label (%) | greedy_prior minus frozen (pp) | sd over subsets (pp) | subsets above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | frozen minus fixed (pp) | greedy_prior minus fixed (pp) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 71.18 | 73.24 | -2.06 | 1.90 | 0 of 20 | -5.78 to +0.97 | +4.72 | +2.66 | KILL |
| L4b | 77.60 | 79.37 | 82.73 | -3.35 | 1.97 | 0 of 20 | -7.24 to -0.35 | +5.12 | +1.77 | KILL |

Split-level gaps above +1.0 pp (of 160 per pool): 11 on L2b, 2 on L4b. Plain fixed and frozen context columns reproduce L2b and L4b at the L6b values (+4.72 and +5.12 for frozen minus fixed).

**Gates (all PASS, in result.json and table.md):** plain fixed at 10% reproduces runs/L2b/result.json and runs/L4b/result.json at 0.000000 pp with the same calls per split. The 3,000-prompt frozen table reproduces runs/L5a/result.json at 0.000000 pp. The per-subset frozen gaps and row positions reproduce runs/L6b/result.json subset_detail at 0.000000 pp on both pools. Spend: 5,376 fixed and frozen checks and 2,560 greedy_prior checks, 0 failures. Every subset has 200 distinct prompts, fixed picks are identical across all tables, and zero eval question texts occur in p0.

**Verdict against the pre-registered rule:** KILL on both pools. The mean over the 20 subset means is -2.06 pp on L2b and -3.35 pp on L4b, both at or below +1.0. No subset on either pool is above +1.0; the best is +0.97 on L2b subset 13. Overall KILL. This is the first test of claim 2 at matched labels, and the learned arm fails it on both pools.

**Diagnostic (not a registered gate):** avrl/exp/l7a_diag_static.py, output runs/L7a/diag_static/result.json. The p0 scorer alone, with no refits and no eval labels, sits -6.01 pp (L2b) and -5.31 pp (L4b) below the frozen table on the same splits and tie seeds. The eval-label refits add +3.95 (L2b) and +1.96 (L4b) on top of it. Most of the loss is therefore in the ranking of the six-feature logistic scorer, not in the refit loop, and the refits recover only part of it. This agrees with G01, where the learned arm trailed recal at 10%.

**What it changes:**
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. At matched labels (the same 200 p0 labels and the same 10% verifier calls per split), the learned arm sits below the label-fitted frozen table on both pools. It does beat plain fixed (+2.66 pp on L2b, +1.77 pp on L4b), so the learned arm carries some gain, but the fixed ranking fitted on 200 labels carries more.
- The G01 kill (label-free greedy against recal) and this kill (greedy initialised from p0 against the frozen 200-label table) point the same way. The learned arm is not the source of the 10% gain. That gain comes from a fixed, label-fitted ranking.

**Caveats:**
- The 160 split-level gaps per pool are not independent: the eight split seeds overlap in eval docs. The replicates that count are the 20 subsets (sd over subset means 1.90 and 1.97).
- The comparison is at equal pre-paid labels (200 p0 labels) and equal verifier calls. The learned arm also uses its verified eval labels to refit, and the frozen arm does not. That difference is the mechanism under test, so it is not a confound to remove.
- These are full-pool results, as registered. The registered L6a run (L4b test rows, pids 0 to 1318) found the 200-label tables below the bar there (see the L6a kill entry), and this run does not test those rows.
- Only the logistic scorer was tried as the learned arm. The diagnostic points at the scorer's ranking, so a scorer with the stratum structure (for example the 36-stratum posterior as a feature) is the obvious next variant. It was not run here.

**Files:** avrl/runs/L7a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag; per pool avrl/runs/L7a/L2b/ and avrl/runs/L7a/L4b/ (result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code); avrl/runs/L7a/diag_static/. Scripts: avrl/exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py, avrl/exp/l7a_combine.py, avrl/exp/l7a_diag_static.py, avrl/exp/L7a.sh.

## L7a 2026-10-18 kill

Hypothesis: at equal labels (200 p0 labels for both arms), the learned online allocator beats the best label-fitted fixed rule. The learned arm is greedy_prior (the G01 greedy_online branch with K = 1: logistic scorer on the six cheap features, initialised on the same 200 p0 prompts, refit every R = max(budget // 8, 40) verified labels on the p0 labels plus all verified eval labels so far). The comparator is the 200-label frozen config B table at 10%. Pass: greedy_prior minus frozen above +1.0 pp on both L2b and L4b. Null: at or below +1.0 pp on at least one pool.

Run: avrl/exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py (a copy of l6b_frozen_label_budget.py; L6b unchanged), launched per pool by avrl/exp/L7a.sh and merged by avrl/exp/l7a_combine.py. CPU only, run in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I, no GPU job. Rollouts are the existing Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct pools on GSM8K (proxy = agreement with group majority, verifier = gold); p0 (3,000 prompts) supplies the 200-label fit. 20 subsets of 200 distinct p0 prompts (numpy RandomState(100 + i)), split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) docs per split. Outputs: avrl/runs/L7a/ (result.json, table.md, run.log, diag_static/).

| pool | plain fixed 10% (%) | greedy_prior (%) | frozen 200-label (%) | greedy_prior minus frozen (pp) | sd over subset means (pp) | subsets above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | frozen minus fixed (pp) | greedy_prior minus fixed (pp) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 71.18 | 73.24 | -2.06 | 1.90 | 0 of 20 | -5.78 to +0.97 | +4.72 | +2.66 | KILL |
| L4b | 77.60 | 79.37 | 82.73 | -3.35 | 1.97 | 0 of 20 | -7.24 to -0.35 | +5.12 | +1.77 | KILL |

Diagnostic, not a registered gate (diag_static): the p0 scorer alone with no refits and no eval labels sits -6.01 pp (L2b) and -5.31 pp (L4b) below the frozen table. The eval-label refits add +3.95 and +1.96 on top. The two parts sum to the headline gaps.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL. Both pools are at or below +1.0 pp (-2.06 on L2b, -3.35 on L4b), and no subset on either pool clears +1.0 (best is +0.97, L2b subset 13). Split-level gaps above +1.0 are 11 of 160 on L2b and 2 of 160 on L4b. All gates PASS. I checked them independently after the run: subset means recomputed from the per-split gaps (max difference 0.0), plain fixed reproduces runs/L2b config B and runs/L4b config B40 per split (1.4e-14 and 0.0 pp), and the per-subset frozen gaps and row positions reproduce runs/L6b subset_detail exactly (0.0 pp).

Note: an earlier "## L7a 2026-10-18 kill (both pools)" entry above was written by the run's own ledger step. It carries the same numbers and gates. This entry is the independent read and adds the follow-ups.

What it changes:
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. This is the first equal-label test of the learned arm, and it fails on both pools. The learned arm beats plain fixed (+2.66 and +1.77 pp), so it carries some gain, but the 200-label frozen table, itself a label-fitted fixed rule, carries more at the same labels.
- Where the gap sits: the static logistic ranking loses about 5 to 6 pp to the table before any refit. The refit loop recovers part of that (+2 to +4 pp). The weak part is the initial ranking, not the online loop. This agrees with G01, where the learned arm also trailed recal at 10%.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. Both arms use rollout-side features only (agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen), so this run gives no prompt-space evidence. L7c is the test of that.

Caveats:
- The 160 split-level gaps per pool are not independent. The replicates that count are the 20 subsets (sd over subset means 1.90 and 1.97).
- The learned arm refits on eval labels and the frozen arm does not. That difference is the mechanism under test, not a confound. The frozen arm also uses its 200 pre-paid labels, as in L6b.
- Full-pool results, as registered. The L4b test rows (pid <= 1318) are not tested here. L6a found 200-label tables below the bar on those rows.
- Only one learned arm was tried (logistic scorer, six cheap features).

Follow-ups (iteration 9, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md):
1. L9a, table-initialised learned arm (CPU, existing feats). Start the greedy loop from the frozen 200-label table's own ranking, not a logistic fit, and refit on p0 plus eval labels with the table posterior as an extra feature. Pass: above +1.0 pp over the 200-label table on both pools. Expected information value high: this is the strongest equal-label form of claim 2. A kill is still the likely result, since the diagnostic puts the loss in the static ranking.
2. L9b, learned arm at 3,000 p0 labels (CPU, existing feats). Fit the logistic scorer on all 3,000 p0 labels and compare with the 3,000-label frozen table (L5a). Pass: above +1.0 pp on both pools. Tells whether the 200-label gap is scorer under-fitting from a small sample or a ceiling of the six-feature logistic family. Expected information value medium-high.
3. L9c, learned arm on the registered test rows (CPU, existing feats). Repeat L7a on L4b rows pid <= 1318 against the L6a 200-label table (+1.22 pp over plain fixed there). Pass: above +1.0 pp on those rows. Closes the caveat above. Expected information value medium; a kill is likely because the comparator is already near the bar.


Critic note (L7a, 2026-10-18). Checked both L7a sections against runs/L7a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag, the per-pool runs/L7a/L2b and runs/L7a/L4b files, runs/L7a/diag_static/, runs/L6b/result.json, runs/L6a, the G01 entry, exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py (a diff against exp/l6b_frozen_label_budget.py and against Sim.greedy_online in exp/g01_label_free_baselines.py), exp/l7a_combine.py, exp/l7a_diag_static.py and exp/L7a.sh. There is no exp/L7a.py; the experiment script is exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py, launched by exp/L7a.sh. Independent replay: exp/l7a_critic_indep.py (own sim split, own frozen table, own plain fixed, own greedy_prior loop; it imports nothing from the experiment modules), log in runs/L7a_critic/indep.log.

What checks out. Every number in both L7a sections matches the raw files: L2b mean gap -2.06 (sd 1.90, 0 of 20 subsets above +1.0, min -5.78, max +0.97 on subset 13), L4b -3.35 (sd 1.97, 0 of 20, min -7.24, max -0.35), plain fixed 68.52 and 77.60, greedy_prior 71.18 and 79.37, frozen 73.24 and 82.73, frozen minus fixed +4.72 and +5.12, greedy_prior minus fixed +2.66 and +1.77, split-level gaps above +1.0 of 11 (L2b) and 2 (L4b) in 160, spend 5,376 and 2,560 checks with 0 failures, diagnostic -6.01 and -5.31 (static p0 scorer minus frozen) plus +3.95 and +1.96 (refits) summing to the headline gaps, max tie-seed spread of greedy hits 0 on both pools. My replay reproduces all 20 subset means on each pool to 4e-15 pp (L2b -2.0633, L4b -3.3523). The rule verdict follows: the pre-registered pass needs a mean above +1.0 on both pools, both means are far below it, and the script applies strict "> 1.0" for PASS and else KILL, so pass and kill are complementary with GATE_FAILED handled separately in the combine step. No leakage: the scorer sees only agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated and log_qlen (no gold, majority, pass_rate, majority_correct, proxy_error or pc columns; no NaNs in them), eval labels enter the refit only for docs already picked and verified, p0 and eval question overlap is 0. Budget is equal: both arms use the same 200 p0 labels and exactly int(0.10 x n_sim) verified eval docs (95 to 101 checks on L4b test-row cuts, 219 to 236 on L2b), so the L6b critic point 5 (frozen arm paying 200 labels beyond the budget) does not apply to the greedy versus frozen contrast. greedy_prior matches G01 greedy_online (K = 1, R = max(budget // 8, 40), top-scored unseen first); the deviations the entry lists (p0 initialisation, max_iter 1000, tie-break by seed draw) are the only ones. Runs are clean: exit codes 0 for both pools and the combine, no warning, ConvergenceWarning or traceback in any run.log, nothing truncated.

Sample size and seeds. The margin to the bar is 3.06 pp (L2b) and 4.35 pp (L4b) against a standard error over the 20 subset means of 0.43 and 0.44, so the label-draw noise cannot flip it. The eval side also cannot: 48 fresh bucket-shuffle cuts of each full pool (seeds 100 to 147, mean over the same 20 tables) give L2b mean -1.80 (sd 0.88, max +0.67, 0 of 48 above +1.0) and L4b -3.65 (sd 0.80, max -1.73, 0 of 48). The eight tie seeds add nothing for greedy_prior (zero spread in hits), so the replicates are the 20 tables and the eval cut, as the entry says.

Corrections (rule verdict unchanged; scope and strength changed).
(1) "The learned arm fails it on both pools" and "sits below the label-fitted frozen table on both pools" are full-pool statements, and on L4b they are carried by the remaining-train rows, as the 3,000-label table's L4b gain was in L6a. On the registered L4b test rows (pid <= 1318, the L6a cut, same 20 tables, splits 0 to 7, 8 tie seeds) greedy_prior minus the 200-label table is +0.37 pp (sd 2.21 over subset means, 9 of 20 subsets above +1.0, min -3.92, max +4.62; my frozen minus fixed there is +1.22, equal to L6a). On 48 fresh test-row cuts it is -0.08 (sd 0.74, 2 of 48 above +1.0). So at test-row scale the learned arm roughly ties a 200-label table that is itself only +1.22 above plain fixed, and greedy_prior minus plain fixed is +1.59 there. The registered rule is on the full pools, so the KILL stands, and even on the test rows the mean is at or below +1.0, but the margin there is 0.6 pp, about 1.3 se. L9c, as proposed, is therefore close to a coin flip on its direction, not "a kill is likely" with room to spare, and any sentence that the learned arm is below the fixed table should carry "on the full pools".
(2) "The learned arm is not the source of the 10% gain" (first entry) is wider than the evidence. L7a tested one learned arm, a six-feature logistic scorer. The online refit of the stratum table (refit B, floor 40) is also an arm that learns from verified labels, with no p0 labels, and it gains +5.02 (L2b) and +5.01 (L4b) over plain fixed, level with the frozen table (L6b critic: -0.29 and +0.12), and 7.08 and 8.36 pp ahead of greedy_prior (result.json greedy_minus_refit_b40_pp, in table.md only as a context column). The defensible reading is narrower: a logistic ranking, even initialised on the same 200 labels and refit on eval labels, loses 2 to 3 pp to a stratum table on the full pools, and the diagnostic puts most of that in the static ranking; nothing tested beats the frozen table, and the best online arm only ties it. That keeps claim 2 not_supported, but it does not show that online learning contributes nothing, and the stratum-posterior variant the entry names as the obvious next arm (L9a) is the test that matters.
(3) Wording and housekeeping, no effect on any number. "The eight tie seeds give identical picks" is shown for hits (spread 0), not for the pick sets. runs/L7a/diag_static/ has no exit_code file (the other run directories do); its run.log shows the diagnostic finished and wrote result.json, and the diagnostic is outside the verdict. There are two L7a sections with the same numbers and verdicts (the run's own and the loop's), as for L6a; one should be removed when the log is next tidied.

Corrected verdict: KILL on both pools, unchanged (greedy_prior minus the 200-label frozen table at 10%: L2b -2.06 pp, L4b -3.35 pp, both at or below +1.0, all gates PASS, reproduced to 4e-15 pp, robust to label draw and to eval-cut resampling). Carry it as: on the full L2b and L4b pools a six-feature logistic greedy learner initialised on the same 200 p0 labels trails the stratum table by 2 to 3 pp, while on the registered L4b test rows it roughly ties it (+0.37, still at or below the bar). Claim 2 stays not_supported as worded, claim 3 stays not_supported.

## L9a 2026-10-18 kill

Hypothesis: a learned online allocator that starts from the frozen 200-label config B table's own ranking (not a logistic fit) and refits on the 200 p0 labels plus verified eval labels beats that same table by more than +1.0 pp at 10% on both L2b and L4b. Null: it does not. This is the strongest equal-label form of claim 2 that was still untested after L7a, which only started from a logistic fit.

What was run: avrl/exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py (L7a and L6b unchanged), launched per pool by avrl/exp/L9a.sh and merged by avrl/exp/l9a_combine.py. CPU only, in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No GPU job, no ssh. Rollouts are the existing Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K pools (L2b, and L4b from the GSM8K test split). Proxy is agreement with the group majority; the label is proxy error against gold. Fit set is p0 (3,000 prompts). 20 subsets of 200 distinct p0 prompts (numpy RandomState(100 + i)), split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds per split, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) verified docs per split: 219 to 236 on L2b (n_sim 2,196 to 2,369) and 198 to 216 on L4b (n_sim 1,989 to 2,162). Arms: frozen (L6b, unchanged), table_zero (greedy from the table with refits off), table_refit (greedy from the table, refit every R = max(budget // 8, 40) = 40 verified labels, giving 4 or 5 refits per split, with StandardScaler plus LogisticRegression(class_weight balanced, max_iter 1000) on six cheap features plus the table posterior). The 200 p0 rows carry a 5-fold out-of-fold table posterior (fold seed 300 + i, chosen in the script because the spec does not fix it). Eval rows carry the full 200-label subset posterior.

| pool | plain fixed 10% (%) | frozen 200-label table (%) | table_refit (%) | table_refit minus frozen (pp) | sd over subset means (pp) | subsets above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | frozen minus fixed (pp) | table_refit minus fixed (pp) | B40 minus fixed (pp, other runs, context) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 73.24 | 72.52 | -0.72 | 1.39 | 3 of 20 | -3.26 to +2.43 | +4.72 | +4.00 | +5.02 | KILL |
| L4b | 77.60 | 82.73 | 81.62 | -1.11 | 1.70 | 3 of 20 | -3.73 to +1.74 | +5.12 | +4.02 | +5.01 | KILL |

Split-level gaps above +1.0 (160 per pool): 18 on L2b, 25 on L4b. The zero-refit arm equals the frozen table on every subset, split and tie seed. Frozen minus fixed reproduces L6b (+4.72, +5.12). The B40 column is the stratum-posterior refit, floor 40, from runs/L3a/fresh (L2b) and runs/L4b (L4b), shown for context only.

Gates, all PASS, re-checked from result.json: plain fixed reproduces runs/L2b config B and runs/L4b config B40 per split (max difference 0.000000 pp); the 3,000-prompt frozen table reproduces runs/L5a (+5.65 and +8.38, exact); per-subset frozen gaps and row positions reproduce runs/L6b exactly; the zero-refit arm equals frozen hits on every subset, split and tie seed; spend has 5,376 fixed and frozen checks and 5,120 table checks with 0 failures (per pool 2,688 and 2,560); each subset has 200 distinct p0 prompts; 0 eval question texts occur in p0.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL. A pass needs a mean above +1.0 pp on both pools. L2b is -0.72 and L4b is -1.11, and 17 of 20 subset means are at or below +1.0 on each pool. Pass and kill are complementary, so there is no inconclusive band. The gates pass, so the verdict is about the arm, not a broken run.

Independent checks (the run report was not trusted as the source): I recomputed the pool means, the sd over subset means (ddof 1), the subset counts and the split counts from result.json, and they match (-0.7225 and -1.1059; sd 1.3895 and 1.7038; 3 and 3 subsets above +1.0; 18 and 25 split gaps above +1.0). Each subset mean equals the mean of its eight split gaps. Exit codes are 0 and done.flag exists. Two corrections to the run report: the L4b frozen hit rate is 82.73 (82.725 rounds up), not 82.72; and the result.json field learned_minus_refit_b40_pp (-5.74 and -6.11) subtracts a B40 gap over plain fixed from a table_refit gap over frozen. It mixes two baselines and should not be cited. The like-for-like comparison is table_refit minus fixed (+4.00, +4.02) against B40 minus fixed (+5.02, +5.01), about -1.0 pp on both pools, though B40 comes from other runs.

What it changes:
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported, and the table start is now tested as well. Starting from the frozen table and refitting makes the ranking worse by 0.7 to 1.1 pp on average, with 17 of 20 subsets at or below the bar on each pool. The learned arm still beats plain fixed (+4.00, +4.02), so it carries gain, but less than the table it starts from. Both learned starts now fail the equal-label test: the logistic start (L7a) and the table start (this run).
- Where the loss sits: the stratum-posterior refit B40, which learns from verified labels with no p0 labels, ties the frozen table (+5.02 against +4.72 on L2b, +5.01 against +5.12 on L4b). The logistic refit, from either start, is 1 to 3 pp below the table. So the learned part that works here is the stratum posterior, and the six-feature logistic is the weak part. The B40 numbers come from other runs and carry their own noise.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. The strata (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated) and the refit features are all rollout-side, so this run gives no prompt-space evidence. L2c, L7c and L8c, the tests that would, have no run directories and have not been run.
- L9b and L9c stay open. L9b (3,000 p0 labels) still bounds the logistic family's ceiling. L9c (registered test rows) is lower priority than L10a: the L7a critic put its direction close to a coin flip.

Caveats:
- A design confound is not ruled out. The 200 p0 rows enter the refit with out-of-fold posteriors built from 160 labels, while eval rows use posteriors built from 200. The logistic model fits the table-feature weight on noisier inputs than it is applied to, which biases the refit against the table. The out-of-fold split is what the spec required to prevent leakage, so this is a design cost, not a bug. The KILL therefore shows that this refit loses to the table under this mismatch. It does not show that a refit can never add gain on top of the table. L10b is the control.
- Refits are few: 4 or 5 per split.
- The replicates that count are the 20 subsets. The 160 split gaps per pool are not independent.
- One learned variant only: a logistic on six cheap features plus the table posterior, R = 40, class_weight balanced. Not tried: fewer features, stronger regularisation, or table-pooled counts (L10a).
- Full pools, as registered. The L4b test rows (pid <= 1318) are not covered here.

Follow-ups (iteration 10, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md):
1. L10a, table-pooled stratum posterior at equal labels (CPU, existing feats). Use the B40 stratum-posterior update, but start it from the frozen 200-label table as prior counts (k0 = 50 per stratum) and add the verified eval labels as counts. No logistic model. Pass: above the frozen 200-label table by more than +1.0 pp on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value high: it tests the one learner that ties the table, at the same labels, with no fitted scorer. A pass would be the first equal-label gain above the table.
2. L10b, matched-precision control for L9a (CPU, existing feats). Rerun L9a unchanged except that each eval row gets the table posterior from one randomly chosen fold table (160 labels), the same precision as the out-of-fold p0 features. Pass: table_refit minus frozen above +1.0 pp on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value medium-high: it decides whether the L9a kill comes from the refit design or from the mismatch the leakage rule forced.
3. L10c, parsimonious refit for the table-start arm (CPU, existing feats). Rerun L9a with the logistic on the table posterior plus agreement and n_distinct only, dropping mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated and log_qlen. Pass: above +1.0 pp on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value medium: with a few dozen proxy-error positives per 200-label subset, seven free coefficients may overfit, and this tests that. A kill would mean overfitting alone does not explain the loss.

Critic note (L9a, 2026-10-18). Checked the L9a section against runs/L9a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag, the per-pool runs/L9a/L2b and runs/L9a/L4b files, runs/L2b, runs/L3a/fresh and runs/L4b (the B40 reference rows), the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py, exp/l9a_combine.py and exp/L9a.sh. There is no exp/L9a.py; the experiment script is exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py, launched by exp/L9a.sh. Independent replay: exp/l9a_critic_indep.py (own sim split, own 200-label table, own 5-fold out-of-fold posterior, own plain fixed and own table-start greedy loop; it imports nothing from the experiment modules), log in runs/L9a_critic/indep.log, plus exp/l9a_critic_subpools.py (log runs/L9a_critic/subpools.log). The entry's "independent checks" re-read result.json only; the replay below rebuilds the numbers from the feats files.

What checks out. Every number in the entry matches the raw files: L2b mean gap -0.72 (sd 1.39, 3 of 20 subsets above +1.0, min -3.26, max +2.43), L4b -1.11 (sd 1.70, 3 of 20, min -3.73, max +1.74), plain fixed 68.52 and 77.60, frozen 73.24 and 82.73 (82.7252), table_refit 72.52 and 81.62, frozen minus fixed +4.72 and +5.12, table_refit minus fixed +4.00 and +4.02, B40 minus fixed +5.02 and +5.01 (the B40 rows in table.md match runs/L3a/fresh config B and runs/L4b config B40 split by split), split-level gaps above +1.0 of 18 (L2b) and 25 (L4b) in 160, spend 5,376 and 5,120 checks with 0 failures, L5a reproduction +5.65 and +8.38, learned_minus_refit_b40_pp -5.74 and -6.11 (and the entry is right that it mixes two baselines). My replay reproduces all 20 subset means on each pool to 2.4e-15 pp and all 160 split gaps per pool to 1.2e-14 pp (L2b -0.7225, sd 1.3895; L4b -1.1059, sd 1.7038), and its zero-refit greedy equals the frozen pick on every run. n_sim is 2,196 to 2,369 (L2b) and 1,989 to 2,162 (L4b), budgets 219 to 236 and 198 to 216, as stated. The rule verdict follows: a pass needs a mean above +1.0 on both pools, both means are far below it, the script applies strict "> 1.0" for PASS and else KILL, so pass and kill are complementary, and GATE_FAILED is handled separately in the combine step. The threshold and metric match the backlog spec (greedy minus the 200-label frozen table at 10%, paired by split and tie seed, mean over 20 subsets x split seeds 0 to 7, sd over subset means). No leakage: the refit sees only agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the table posterior (no gold, majority, pass_rate, majority_correct, proxy_error or pc columns; no NaNs in the six features in p0, L2b or L4b, so fillna(0) changes nothing); each p0 row's posterior comes from a table that excludes that row's label; eval rows are scored by the table fitted on the 200 p0 labels only; eval labels enter the refit only for docs already picked and verified; p0 and eval question overlap is 0 exact and 0 after lower-casing and stripping punctuation. Budget is equal for the contrast under test: frozen and table_refit use the same 200 p0 labels and exactly int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct verified docs per split (gate, 0 failures). Runs are clean: exit codes 0 for both pools and the combine, no warning, error, traceback or nan in run.log or either per-pool log, and the script (22:34:22) predates the runs (22:36:43, 22:38:33).

Sample size and seeds. The margin to the bar is 1.72 pp (L2b) and 2.11 pp (L4b) against a standard error over the 20 subset means of 0.31 and 0.38, about 5.5 se on each pool, so the label-draw noise cannot flip it. The eval side cannot either: 48 fresh bucket-shuffle cuts of each full pool (seeds 100 to 147, tie seeds 0 to 2, mean over the same 20 tables) give L2b -0.52 (sd 0.61, min -1.63, max +1.34, 1 of 48 above +1.0) and L4b -1.11 (sd 0.58, min -2.50, max +0.11, 0 of 48). The eight split seeds reshuffle buckets of one pool, so the replicates that count are the 20 tables and the eval cut, as the entry says.

Corrections (rule verdict unchanged; scope and wording changed).
(1) Pool description. "L4b from the GSM8K test split" is wrong. The L4b pool is 2,792 prompts: the 1,319 test rows (pid <= 1318) plus 1,473 remaining train rows (L4b section, deviation from the registered test-only pool). The entry's last caveat ("the L4b test rows are not covered here") is right, but it leaves the reader with the wrong picture of what the L4b number is.
(2) The loss is a full-pool result and it reverses on the registered test rows. Same 20 tables, splits 0 to 7, 8 tie seeds, buckets from the cell100 column of runs/L4b/feats.parquet (stand-in, as in L6a and the L7a critic): on the L4b test rows (pid <= 1318, 1,319 rows, budgets 95 to 101, 2 refits per run) table_refit minus the 200-label frozen table is +1.44 pp (sd 2.01 over subset means, 11 of 20 subsets above +1.0, min -2.67, max +4.62; my frozen minus fixed there is +1.22, equal to L6a; table_refit minus fixed +2.66). On 48 fresh test-row cuts it is +1.16 (sd 0.79, min -0.47, max +2.92, 22 of 48 above +1.0). On the remaining-train rows (pid > 1318, 1,473 rows, budgets 111 to 117) it is -0.96 (sd 3.03, 6 of 20 above +1.0; fresh cuts -0.63, 0 of 48 above; frozen minus fixed +8.58). The sub-pool values are not a weighted average of the full-pool -1.11, because buckets and budgets differ, and the test-row margin over the bar is +0.44 pp, about 1 se (2.01 / sqrt(20) = 0.45), so this is level with the bar, not a pass. But it means "starting from the frozen table and refitting makes the ranking worse by 0.7 to 1.1 pp on average" and "both learned starts now fail the equal-label test" hold on the full pools only. At test-row scale the logistic start (L7a critic: +0.37) ties the table and the table start (+1.44) sits above the bar by a point estimate. L9c as the entry ranks it ("lower priority than L10a: the L7a critic put its direction close to a coin flip") rests on the logistic start. For the table start the direction is a coin flip with a positive lean, and it is the only learned-arm reading so far that lands above +1.0. A table-start run on the registered test rows should be pre-registered as its own test, not read from this stand-in.
(3) "The learned arm still beats plain fixed (+4.00, +4.02), so it carries gain" is equal in verifier calls only. table_refit also uses 200 pre-paid p0 labels that plain fixed does not (L6a critic point 3). The sentence should read "at equal verifier calls, with 200 extra pre-paid labels".
(4) "The logistic refit, from either start, is 1 to 3 pp below the table" is 0.7 to 3.4 pp (L7a -2.06 and -3.35, L9a -0.72 and -1.11). "The six-feature logistic is the weak part" overstates what was tested: the L9a refit has seven features (the table posterior is the seventh), its loss is confounded by the 160-label versus 200-label posterior mismatch the entry already lists, and per (2) it does not hold at test-row scale. B40 only ties the frozen table, so "the learned part that works" means "ties the table with no p0 labels".
(5) Housekeeping, no effect on any number. "4 or 5 refits per split" is 5 on every L2b split and 4 on two L4b splits (budgets 198 and 199), 5 on the other six. The tie-seed spread of table_refit hits reaches 12 (L2b) and 10 (L4b) hits on one split, about 5 pp of a single split's hit rate, and is not stated in the entry; it is averaged out by the eight tie seeds in the metric, and the fresh-cut check above uses three.

Corrected verdict: KILL on both pools, unchanged (table_refit minus the 200-label frozen table at 10%: L2b -0.72 pp, L4b -1.11 pp, both at or below +1.0, all gates PASS, reproduced to 1e-14 pp, 5.5 se below the bar, robust to label draw and to eval-cut resampling). Carry it as: on the full L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts, 47% test rows) pools a table-start logistic refit trails the 200-label stratum table by 0.7 to 1.1 pp, while on the registered L4b test rows a stand-in replay puts it at +1.44 (fresh cuts +1.16), level with the bar. Claim 2 stays not_supported as worded, claim 3 stays not_supported. L9c should be re-scoped to the table start before it is ranked below L10a.

## L10a 2026-10-18 kill (L2b kill at +0.33 pp, L4b pass at +1.30 pp; overall kill)

Hypothesis: a stratum-posterior learner that starts from the frozen 200-label config B table as prior counts (k0 = 50 per stratum) and adds the verified eval labels as counts beats that same table by more than +1.0 pp at 10% on both L2b and L4b. Null: it does not. This is the one equal-label learner still untested that fits no scorer. B40 (stratum posterior, no p0 labels) had tied the frozen table, so a table-prior version could land either side of the bar.

What was run: the existing Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollout pools. Proxy is agreement with the group majority; the label is proxy error against gold. Fit set is p0 (3,000 prompts). Eval pools: L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts, of which 1,319 are the registered test rows; the full pool is used here). Script avrl/exp/l10a_table_pooled_posterior.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py (L9a, L7a and L6b unchanged), launched per pool by avrl/exp/L10a.sh and merged by avrl/exp/l10a_combine.py. CPU only, in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No GPU job, no gpu_run.sh, no ssh, no pip. 20 subsets of 200 distinct p0 prompts (numpy RandomState(100 + i)), split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) verified docs per split, the same simulated pools and budgets as L9a (219 to 236 docs on L2b, 198 to 216 on L4b), since plain fixed reproduces the reference picks call for call. Arms: plain fixed (the fixed ranking of runs/L2b and runs/L4b); frozen (L6b config B, fitted once on each subset's 200 labels, never refit); table_pooled (greedy from the same table, per-stratum score (e + 50 x table_post) / (v + 50), where e and v are the verified eval errors and count so far, recomputed every R = max(budget // 8, 40) = 40 verified labels; no logistic model, no p0 refit).

| pool | plain fixed (%) | frozen 200-label (%) | table_pooled (%) | table_pooled minus frozen (pp) | sd over subset means (pp) | subsets above +1.0 | split gaps above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | frozen minus fixed (pp) | table_pooled minus fixed (pp) | B40 minus fixed (pp, other runs, context) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 73.24 | 73.58 | +0.33 | 1.57 | 7 of 20 | 53 of 160 | -1.45 to +3.61 | +4.72 | +5.06 | +5.02 | KILL |
| L4b | 77.60 | 82.73 | 84.02 | +1.30 | 1.65 | 12 of 20 | 87 of 160 | -1.45 to +3.98 | +5.12 | +6.42 | +5.01 | PASS |

Gates, all PASS, re-checked from result.json: plain fixed reproduces runs/L2b config B and runs/L4b config B40 (max diff 0.000 pp, calls match); the 3,000-prompt frozen table reproduces runs/L5a (+5.65 on L2b, +8.38 on L4b); per-subset frozen gaps reproduce runs/L6b (row positions match); the zero-refit arm equals frozen hits on every subset, split and tie seed; spend has 5,376 fixed and frozen checks and 5,120 table checks, 0 failures; each subset has 200 distinct p0 prompts; 0 eval question texts occur in p0.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL. Per pool, L2b is KILL (+0.33, at or below +1.0) and L4b is PASS (+1.30, above +1.0). A pass needs both pools above +1.0, so the overall verdict is KILL. The L4b pass clears the bar by 0.30 pp, which is 0.8 standard errors of the 20-subset mean (0.37). Its normal-approximation 95% interval over label draws is +0.57 to +2.02, so it contains the bar and should not be read as a settled pass. Pass and kill are complementary, so there is no inconclusive band. The gates pass, so the verdict is about the arm, not a broken run.

Independent checks: the run report was not taken as the source. I read result.json, table.md, run.log, both per-pool logs, exit_code (0 for both pools and the combine) and done.flag. From the raw per-split gaps (gap_pp_per_subset, 20 subsets x 8 splits per pool) I recomputed the pool means (+0.3343 and +1.2960), the sd over subset means with ddof 1 (1.5724 and 1.6524), the subset counts above +1.0 (7 and 12) and the split counts above +1.0 (53 and 87 of 160). Each subset mean equals the mean of its eight split gaps (max difference 0). The pool means also equal the differences of the stored hit rates (73.576 minus 73.242; 84.021 minus 82.725). All match the run report.

Caveats:
- The 20 subsets are the only replicates the rule uses. Eval-side resampling is not run here (see L11c).
- Tie-break spread within a split is large: pooled hits vary across the eight tie-break seeds by up to 16 hits on L2b and 19 on L4b (result.json max_tie_seed_spread_hits). Each split gap is averaged over those seeds, so the rule is unaffected, but the split-level counts are noisy.
- One variant only: k0 = 50 and R = 40, not swept (see L11b).
- The B40 column comes from other runs (runs/L3a/fresh for L2b, runs/L4b for L4b) and carries their noise. table.md omits the B40 reference row that L9a had, since the spec did not list it; the numbers stay in runs/L4b.
- L4b is 47% registered test rows, and this run uses the full pool. The registered test-row cut has not been run for this learner.

What it changes:
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. This is the third equal-label learned arm tested against the 200-label frozen table, and the first that fits no scorer. None passes on both pools: the logistic from scratch (L7a) is -2.06 and -3.35, the logistic table start (L9a) is -0.72 and -1.11, and the stratum posterior from the table (L10a) is +0.33 and +1.30. Equal budget here means the same 200 prepaid p0 labels and the same 10% verifier calls for both arms, with the table arm's eval labels entering only as counts. What the data support: online stratum-posterior updating adds about +0.3 pp (L2b, interval contains zero) to +1.3 pp (L4b) over the frozen 200-label table at equal verifier calls, and it sits +5.06 and +6.42 above plain fixed. That is a small gain above the table, not one that clears the pre-registered bar on both pools.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. The stratum key is still agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated, all rollout-side, so this run gives no prompt-space evidence. L2c, L7c and L8c remain untested.
- Carry it as: on the full L2b and L4b pools, a table-prior stratum posterior at 10% verifier calls sits +0.33 and +1.30 pp above the 200-label frozen table and does not clear +1.0 on both pools. The L4b point estimate is above the bar, but the label-draw interval contains it.

Follow-ups (iteration 11, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md):
1. L11a, table_pooled on the registered L4b test rows (CPU, existing feats). The L10a arm and rule on the 1,319 test rows (pid <= 1318), cut before the cell100 split as in L9c. Pass: mean gap above +1.0 pp over 20 subsets. Kill: at or below +1.0. Expected information value high: it is the only registered cut no equal-label learner has been run on, the full-pool L4b margin is 0.3 pp, and the L9a critic's stand-in replay gave the table-start logistic +1.44 on these rows, so the cut may favour table-start learners. It needs its own registration.
2. L11b, k0 and refit-cadence sensitivity (CPU, existing feats). Vary k0 in {10, 200} at R = 40, and R in {20, 80} at k0 = 50, on L2b and L4b, with R exposed as a flag in a copy of the L10a script. The grid is sensitivity only. Pass: a setting above +1.0 pp on both pools is named as a candidate for a fresh-pool test, not as a claim pass. Kill: no setting clears +1.0 on both pools. Expected information value medium: it tells whether the L2b kill comes from prior strength or from a ceiling of the posterior learner.
3. L11c, fresh eval-cut replicate of L10a (CPU, existing feats). The L10a arms on 48 fresh bucket-shuffle cuts of each full pool (seeds 100 to 147, tie seeds 0 to 2, mean over the same 20 tables), as the L9a critic ran for the logistic start. Pass: mean over cuts above +1.0 pp on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value medium-high: the 20 label draws cannot test eval-side resampling, and the L4b margin is 0.8 standard errors.

Critic note (L10a, 2026-10-18). Checked the L10a section against runs/L10a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag, the per-pool runs/L10a/L2b and runs/L10a/L4b files, the B40 reference rows (runs/L3a/fresh, runs/L4b), runs/L7a and runs/L9a result.json, the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md (L10a), exp/l10a_table_pooled_posterior.py, exp/l10a_combine.py and exp/L10a.sh. There is no exp/L10a.py; the experiment script is exp/l10a_table_pooled_posterior.py, launched by exp/L10a.sh. Independent replay: exp/l10a_critic_indep.py (own sim split, own 200-label table, own plain fixed and own pooled greedy loop; it imports nothing from the experiment modules), log runs/L10a_critic/indep.log, plus exp/l10a_critic_subpools.py (log runs/L10a_critic/subpools.log).

What checks out. Every number in the entry matches the raw files: L2b mean gap +0.3343 (sd 1.5724, 7 of 20 subsets above +1.0, 53 of 160 split gaps, min -1.45, max +3.61), L4b +1.2960 (sd 1.6524, 12 of 20, 87 of 160, min -1.45, max +3.98), plain fixed 68.52 and 77.60, frozen 73.24 and 82.73, table_pooled 73.58 and 84.02, frozen minus fixed +4.72 and +5.12, table_pooled minus fixed +5.06 and +6.42, B40 minus fixed +5.02 and +5.01, L5a reproduction +5.65 and +8.38, tie-seed spread 16 and 19 hits, budgets 219 to 236 and 198 to 216, spend checks 5,376 and 5,120 summed over the two pools (2,688 and 2,560 per pool) with 0 failures, 0 eval question overlap with p0 (also 0 after lower-casing and stripping punctuation), and the interval +0.57 to +2.02 and 0.8 se for L4b. L7a (-2.06, -3.35) and L9a (-0.72, -1.11) are quoted correctly. My replay reproduces all 20 subset means on each pool to 1.8e-15 pp and all 160 split gaps per pool to 7.1e-15 pp, and its zero-update greedy equals the frozen pick on every run. The verdict follows from the registered rule: per pool PASS only if the mean is above +1.0 (L2b +0.33 is KILL, L4b +1.30 is PASS), overall PASS needs both, so overall KILL, with no inconclusive band. The registered spec (k0 = 50, no logistic, same 20 subsets, splits 0 to 7, 8 tie seeds, budget 0.10, floor 40) is what the script runs, and the script (22:52:16) predates the runs (22:52:49 and 22:53:10). No leakage: the allocator reads agreement, n_distinct and any_truncated plus proxy_error of docs already verified; the 200 p0 labels enter only through the table; a probe that permutes the labels of all unverified eval docs leaves every pick sequence unchanged (0 of 4 runs changed on each pool). Budget is equal across policies: fixed, frozen, table_zero and table_pooled each take exactly int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs on every run (gate, 0 failures). Logs are clean: exit codes 0, no warning, error, traceback or nan.

Sample size and seeds. Per-pool numbers are stable under eval-side resampling. 48 fresh bucket-shuffle cuts of each full pool (seeds 100 to 147, tie seeds 0 to 2, same 20 tables): L2b +0.35 (sd 0.50, min -1.17, max +1.45, 5 of 48 above +1.0), L4b +1.30 (sd 0.47, min +0.26, max +2.27, 35 of 48 above +1.0). The binding noise is the label draw, not the eval cut.

Corrections (rule verdict unchanged; scope and wording changed).
(1) The L4b PASS is a full-pool result and does not hold on either component. Same 20 tables, splits 0 to 7, 8 tie seeds, buckets from the cell100 column (stand-in, as in the L9a critic): on the L4b registered test rows (pid <= 1318, 1,319 rows, budgets 95 to 101, 2 updates per run) table_pooled minus the frozen table is +0.34 pp (sd 0.84 over subset means, 6 of 20 subsets above +1.0, min -1.00, max +1.95; frozen minus fixed +1.22, equal to L6a; table_pooled minus fixed +1.56), 3.5 se below the bar; on 48 fresh test-row cuts +0.50 (sd 0.50, 7 of 48 above +1.0). On the remaining-train rows (pid > 1318, 1,473 rows, budgets 111 to 117) it is +0.50 (sd 1.68, 4 of 20 above +1.0, min -1.69, max +4.62; frozen minus fixed +8.58); fresh cuts +0.71 (10 of 48 above +1.0). The sub-pool values are not a weighted average of the full-pool +1.30 (buckets and budgets differ), and I did not test why the 2,792-row mix scores higher than both parts. So "L4b pass at +1.30" is true only for the mixed 2,792-prompt pool; the entry's carry-forward ("the L4b point estimate is above the bar, but the label-draw interval contains it") should add that neither registered sub-pool reaches the bar.
(2) L2b is not a decisive kill either. The normal-approximation 95% interval over label draws is -0.35 to +1.02 (se 0.35, z = -1.9 against the bar), so it contains the bar at its upper edge as well as zero. The entry says only that it contains zero. The KILL stands as the rule applies it (point estimate at or below +1.0), and the fresh-cut check (+0.35) agrees, but both pools sit within about two label-draw se of the bar, so the equal-label gain over the table is between 0 and about +1.3 pp and not tightly pinned.
(3) L11a's rationale does not transfer. The entry ranks L11a "high" partly because the L9a critic's stand-in gave the table-start logistic +1.44 on the test rows, "so the cut may favour table-start learners". The same stand-in replay for the L10a arm gives +0.34 (fresh cuts +0.50), 1.1 pp below that logistic reading and below the bar. L11a is still worth its own registration (the stand-in is not the registered cut), but a kill is the likely outcome, so its expected information value is medium, not high.
(4) "it sits +5.06 and +6.42 above plain fixed" follows the phrase "at equal verifier calls", but table_pooled also uses 200 pre-paid p0 labels that plain fixed does not (L6a critic point 3, L9a critic correction 3). Equal in verifier calls, with 200 extra pre-paid labels. Against the zero-label B40 (+5.02 and +5.01 above fixed, other runs) the 200 labels as prior counts add about +0.04 pp on L2b and +1.41 pp on L4b, the second figure again resting on the mixed pool.
(5) Housekeeping, no effect on any number. The "5,376 fixed and frozen checks and 5,120 table checks" are sums over both pools (2,688 and 2,560 per pool). The pooled prior is k0 = 50 pseudo-counts at the table posterior per stratum, so the 200 p0 labels enter with weight 50 per stratum, not their actual per-stratum counts; "table_pooled" names that rule, not a pooling of all raw counts, and L11b's k0 grid is where the weight is varied. Refits are 5 per run on L2b and 4 or 5 on L4b.
