Corrected verdict: KILL overall, unchanged (table_pooled minus the 200-label frozen table at 10%: L2b +0.33 pp KILL, L4b +1.30 pp PASS, overall needs both; all gates PASS; reproduced to 1e-14 pp; no leakage; equal budget). Carry it as: on the full L2b (3,000 prompts) and mixed L4b (2,792 prompts) pools a table-prior stratum posterior sits +0.33 and +1.30 above the 200-label table, each within about two label-draw se of the bar; on the registered L4b test rows a stand-in replay gives +0.34 and on the remaining-train rows +0.50, both below +1.0. Claim 2 stays not_supported as worded, claim 3 stays not_supported. L11a stays a legitimate separate registration but is expected to kill; L11c (fresh eval cuts) is largely answered for the full pools by the 48-cut replay above (+0.35 and +1.30, 5 and 35 of 48 above +1.0), so it adds less than the entry assumes unless it also covers the sub-pools.

## L11a 2026-10-18 kill (table_pooled on the 1,319 registered L4b test rows, +0.34 pp)

Hypothesis: on the 1,319 registered L4b test rows (pid <= 1318), the L10a table-pooled stratum posterior at 10% budget (the frozen 200-label config B table as prior counts, k0 = 50, greedy online verification, refit every R = 40 verified labels, no logistic model) beats the frozen 200-label table by more than +1.0 pp of hit rate. Null: it does not. This is the one equal-label learner cut that had not yet been run under its own registration.

What was run: existing Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts, no new generation. Proxy is agreement with the group majority; the label is proxy error against gold. Fit set is p0 (3,000 train prompts); each subset draws 200 of them with numpy RandomState(100 + i). Eval set is runs/L4b/feats.parquet cut to pid <= 1318 before the cell100 split (1,319 rows, proxy error 26.23%). Script avrl/exp/l11a_table_pooled_test_rows.py is a copy of the L10a script with an --eval_pid_max flag. The L10a script was not edited in this run (its mtime predates the run). Launched by avrl/exp/L11a.sh, CPU only through /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No GPU job, no gpu_run.sh, no ssh, no pip, no p0 edits. 20 subsets of 200 distinct p0 prompts, split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) verified docs per split (95 to 101 docs on these rows). Output: avrl/runs/L11a/ (result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, done.flag).

| arm (registered test rows, 1,319) | hit rate (%) | gap vs plain fixed (pp) | gap vs frozen 200-label (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|
| plain fixed (no labels) | 84.50 | 0 | n/a |
| frozen 200-label config B table | 85.73 | +1.22 | 0 |
| table_pooled (L11a arm: 200 prior labels, plus online verified labels) | 86.07 | +1.56 | +0.34 |
| reference, frozen 3,000-label table on the same rows (not equal labels, from L6a) | n/a | +3.39 | n/a |

| table_pooled minus frozen 200-label, 20 subsets | value |
|---|---|
| mean over the 20 subset means | +0.34 pp |
| sd over subset means (ddof 1) | 0.84 pp |
| standard error of the mean (sd / sqrt 20) | 0.19 pp |
| subsets above +1.0 | 6 of 20 |
| split-level gaps above +1.0 | 36 of 160 |
| min to max subset mean | -1.00 to +1.95 pp |
| mean gap by split seed 0 to 7 | +0.31, +0.59, -0.04, +0.03, +0.03, +1.23, +0.22, +0.37 |

For context, the same L10a arm on the full L4b pool (2,792 prompts, 47% of them registered test rows) gave +1.30 pp in the L10a entry. That pass does not carry to the registered rows.

Gates, all PASS in run.log: the 3,000-prompt frozen table on the test rows reproduces runs/L6a exactly (max abs diff 0.0); per-subset frozen gaps and row positions match runs/L6a; the zero-update arm equals the frozen table; spend gates pass with 2,688 and 2,560 checks and 0 failures; each subset has 200 distinct prompts; 0 eval question texts occur in p0.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL. The pass rule is a mean above +1.0 pp. The mean is +0.34, which is 0.66 pp below the bar and 3.5 standard errors below it, so this is not a borderline call. Pass and kill are complementary, so there is no inconclusive band. The gates pass, so the verdict is about the arm, not a broken run.

Independent checks: the run report was not taken as the source. I read result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code (0) and done.flag. From the raw per-split gaps in subset_detail I recomputed the mean (+0.3416), the sd over subset means (0.8368, ddof 1), the counts (6 of 20 subsets, 36 of 160 splits) and the per-seed means. Each subset mean equals the mean of its eight split gaps and the stored subset_mean_pp (max difference 0). The frozen-minus-fixed (+1.2223) and table_pooled-minus-fixed (+1.5639) means match the stored values. I diffed the new script against the L10a script: the only analysis change is the pid cut in load_eval, applied before the split, and the reproduction references point at runs/L6a. The eval rows have pids 0 to 1318 with 200 distinct pids in every subset. Pids repeat across the two files because they are row indices in each file, so the leakage check that counts is question text (0 shared). I did not re-derive n_sim per split; the spend numbers are the script's own gate, and the budget range matches the L10a critic's stand-in.

Deviation from the registration (disclosed in the run report): the spec's reproduction references (runs/L2b, L4b, L5a, L6b) are full-frame and cannot match a pid cut. The gates use runs/L6a, which holds the same 1,319 rows and the same subset draws, and all three gates match it exactly. Arms, thresholds, seeds, k0 and budget are unchanged.

Caveat: the registered test rows have now been used in several verdicts (L6a, the L10a critic's stand-in, and this run). Further claims on them carry forking risk, so new claims need fresh rows.

What it changes:
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. On the registered rows, the L10a arm, the strongest equal-label learner on the full pools, gains only +0.34 pp over the 200-label table it starts from, and +1.56 pp over plain fixed. The online updating adds little above a fixed table on these rows.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. The strata are rollout-side (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated), so this run gives no prompt-space evidence.
- Carry it as: on the registered GSM8K test rows, online stratum-posterior updating over the frozen 200-label table adds +0.34 pp at 10% verifier calls, 3.5 standard errors below the +1.0 bar. The 200-label config B gain on these rows is +1.22 pp, against +5.12 pp on the full L4b pool, so any 200-label gain claim must state the test-row figure beside the full-pool one.

Follow-ups (iteration 12, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 12 (after L11a)"):
1. L12a, k0 and R sensitivity on the 1,473 remaining-train rows as a development set (CPU, existing feats). Expected information value medium. It separates "prior too strong" from "a ceiling for the posterior learner" without spending the registered rows on a grid. A dev setting above +1.0 pp earns one pre-registered run on the test rows; it is not a claim pass.
2. L12b, why the 200-label table's gain differs by row set (CPU, existing feats, no new arm). The frozen-table gain is +1.22 pp on the test rows and about +8.6 pp on the remaining-train rows (the L10a critic's stand-in, not re-derived here). Compare per-stratum error rates, occupancy and rank order across the two row sets. Pass: Spearman correlation of the 36 stratum error rates above 0.8. Kill: below 0.5. Expected information value high for claim 3: if the stratum order does not transfer, the table's gain is not a stable property of prompt-space structure.
3. L12c, fresh held-out rows on a second task (GPU rollouts through gpu_run.sh, CPU analysis). Expected information value high: the GSM8K registered rows have been used in several verdicts, and only a new task gives uncontaminated rows. The design needs a fit pool and a held-out eval pool. MATH-500 has only 500 problems, so the fit pool would need the MATH train split (not yet checked), and no MATH answer extractor exists (p0_rollouts.py extracts only the GSM8K #### answer). Pass: table_pooled minus the frozen table above +1.0 pp, mean over 20 subsets on the held-out rows. Kill: at or below +1.0 pp.

Checked the L11a section against runs/L11a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code and done.flag, runs/L6a/result.json (reference), runs/L10a/result.json, runs/L10a_critic/indep.log, the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md (L11a, and the iteration 12 follow-ups), and a line-by-line diff of exp/l11a_table_pooled_test_rows.py against exp/l10a_table_pooled_posterior.py. What checks out. Every number in the entry matches the raw files: mean +0.3416 (sd 0.8368 ddof 1, se 0.187), 6 of 20 subsets above +1.0, 36 of 160 split gaps, min -1.00, max +1.95, per-split-seed means +0.31, +0.59, -0.04, +0.03, +0.03, +1.23, +0.22, +0.37, plain fixed 84.50, frozen 85.73, table_pooled 86.07, frozen minus fixed +1.22, table_pooled minus fixed +1.56, the L6a 3,000-label reference +3.39, 1,319 rows, proxy error 26.23%, spend checks 2,688 and 2,560 with 0 failures, 0 eval question texts in p0 (also 0 after lower-casing and stripping punctuation; 3,000 distinct p0 questions, 1,319 distinct eval questions). My replay reproduces all 20 subset means to 1.1e-15 pp and all 160 split gaps to 6.3e-15 pp, with budgets 95, 97, 99, 99, 99, 100, 101, 101 (n_sim 957 to 1,017) and exactly 2 refits per run. The verdict follows from the registered rule: the mean +0.34 is at or below +1.0, so KILL, and there is no inconclusive band. The script (23:09:57) and L11a.sh (23:10:05) predate the outputs (23:10:12), and the L10a script (22:52:15) is unedited. No leakage: the allocator reads agreement, n_distinct and any_truncated (all rollout-side; agreement is the majority-vote share, no gold) plus proxy_error of docs already verified; the 200 p0 labels enter only through the table; permuting the labels of all unverified eval docs leaves every pick sequence unchanged (0 of 72 comparisons), and the same holds with gold, pass_rate, majority_correct, majority and question removed from the frame (0 of 72). Budget is equal across policies: fixed, frozen, table_zero and table_pooled each take exactly int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs on every run, and the zero-update arm equals the frozen pick on every run. run.log is clean (exit code 0; the only keyword match is the words "proxy error" in the first line).

Corrections (rule verdict unchanged; scope and wording changed).
(1) L11a is not independent of the L10a critic's stand-in. The entry says only that "the budget range matches the L10a critic's stand-in", and the L10a critic note (3) said "the stand-in is not the registered cut". It is the same cut: both use the cell100 column as the bucket, cut pid <= 1318 before the split, the same 20 tables, splits 0 to 7 and 8 tie seeds. The registered run reproduces the stand-in on every figure it reported (+0.34, sd 0.84, 6 of 20, min -1.00, max +1.95, frozen minus fixed +1.22, table_pooled minus fixed +1.56, budgets 95 to 101, 2 updates per run) and my replay matches both to 1e-15 pp. So L11a is a deterministic registered re-execution that confirms the stand-in, not a second measurement, and the "expected information value high" ranking given at registration was already spent by the L10a critic note. The independent corroboration is the 48 fresh test-row cuts in runs/L10a_critic/indep.log (3b): +0.50 (sd 0.50, min -0.35, max +2.10, 7 of 48 above +1.0).
(2) "3.5 standard errors below the bar, not a borderline call" counts the label-draw (table) variance only. The 20 subsets share the same 8 split seeds, so the bucket-shuffle (eval cut) variance is common to all of them and is not in the 0.84 sd. The mean gap over the 20 tables differs by split seed (sd 0.42 across the 8 seeds, from +1.23 to -0.04). A subset x split variance decomposition gives a combined se of 0.23 for the grand mean, so z = -2.9 against the bar; using the fresh-cut sd of 0.50 per cut instead gives se 0.26 and z = -2.6. The KILL stands (the rule is on the realized mean, +0.34, and the fresh-cut mean of +0.50 agrees), and a miss of the bar by 2.6 to 2.9 combined se is still a clear one, but carry it as "about 2.6 to 2.9 se below the bar counting both label and cut noise", not 3.5.
(3) Housekeeping, no effect on any number. "The eval rows have pids 0 to 1318 with 200 distinct pids in every subset" mixes two sets: the 200 distinct pids per subset are p0 prompts (row positions in p0), and the eval rows are the 1,319 L4b pids. "The spec's reproduction references" were not in the L11a spec (it lists none); they are inherited from the L10a script's gates (L2b, L4b, L5a, L6b), and the copy swaps them for runs/L6a, which is the right reference for these rows and matches exactly. The run's max tie-seed spread is 9 hits (result.json max_tie_seed_spread_hits), not mentioned in the entry; each split gap averages over 8 tie seeds, so the rule is unaffected.

Corrected verdict: KILL, unchanged (table_pooled minus the 200-label frozen table on the 1,319 registered L4b test rows: mean +0.34 pp, at or below +1.0; 6 of 20 subsets above the bar; all gates PASS; reproduced to 1e-14 pp; no leakage; equal budget). Carry it as: +0.34 pp, about 2.6 to 2.9 combined se below the +1.0 bar (3.5 on label-draw se alone), identical to the L10a critic's stand-in, so it adds a registered confirmation rather than new evidence. Claims 2 and 3 stay not_supported. The follow-ups L12a to L12c are unaffected.

## L10b 2026-10-18 kill

Hypothesis: if each eval row gets its table posterior at the same precision as the out-of-fold p0 features (the 160-label fold table, not the 200-label subset table), the table-start logistic refit beats the 200-label frozen table by more than +1.0 pp at 10% on both L2b and L4b. Null: it does not. This is the control for L9a. If the L9a kill came from the 160 vs 200 label posterior mismatch, the matched arm should move up.

What was run: avrl/exp/l10b_matched_precision.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py (L9a unchanged), launched per pool by avrl/exp/L10b.sh and merged by avrl/exp/l10b_combine.py, which also runs avrl/exp/l10b_check.py. CPU only, in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No gpu_run.sh, no ssh, no pip, no new rollouts. Model and rollouts: the existing Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K pools. Proxy is agreement with the group majority; the label is proxy error against gold. Fit set is p0 (3,000 prompts). Eval pools: L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts, 47% registered test rows; full pool, as in L9a and L10a). 20 subsets of 200 distinct p0 prompts (RandomState(100 + i)), split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim): 219 to 236 verified docs on L2b and 198 to 216 on L4b. Refit every R = max(budget // 8, 40) = 40 verified labels, 4 or 5 refits per split, StandardScaler plus LogisticRegression(class_weight balanced, max_iter 1000) on six cheap features plus the posterior. Assumption, not in the spec: each eval row draws one fold f in 0 to 4 from numpy RandomState(400 + i) and is scored by the 160-label table fitted without fold f. The frozen comparator is the 200-label subset table, unchanged.

| pool | plain fixed 10% (%) | frozen 200-label (%) | table_refit, matched start (%) | table_refit minus frozen (pp) | sd over subset means (pp) | subsets above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | L9a table_refit minus frozen (pp) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 73.24 | 72.53 | -0.71 | 1.23 | 2 of 20 | -3.15 to +1.71 | -0.72 | KILL |
| L4b | 77.60 | 82.73 | 81.54 | -1.19 | 1.67 | 2 of 20 | -3.74 to +1.94 | -1.11 | KILL |

Context, same subsets and tie seeds:

| pool | table_zero minus frozen (pp, matched start, refits off) | table_refit minus table_zero (pp) | subsets where refit is below its zero-refit start | split gaps above +1.0 (of 160; L9a 18 and 25) | frozen minus fixed (pp) | table_refit minus fixed (pp) | L9a table_refit minus fixed (pp) | paired change vs L9a per subset, mean (sd) (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | +0.36 | -1.07 | 18 of 20 | 16 | +4.72 | +4.02 | +4.00 | +0.01 (0.30) |
| L4b | -0.08 | -1.11 | 16 of 20 | 23 | +5.12 | +3.93 | +4.02 | -0.08 (0.35) |

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL on both pools. A pass needs a mean above +1.0 pp on both pools. L2b is -0.71 and L4b is -1.19, 1.7 and 2.2 pp below the bar. Pass and kill are complementary, so there is no inconclusive band. The gates pass, so the verdict is about the arm, not a broken run. Gates: plain fixed reproduces runs/L2b config B and runs/L4b config B40 (max difference 0.000 pp); the 3,000-prompt frozen table reproduces runs/L5a (+5.65, +8.38); per-subset frozen gaps reproduce runs/L6b exactly; the matched zero-refit gate passes on both pools; spend has 5,376 fixed and frozen checks and 5,120 table checks with 0 failures; each subset has 200 distinct p0 prompts; 0 eval question texts occur in p0.

Independent checks (the run report was not taken as the source): I read result.json, the combined run.log, both per-pool logs, exit_code (0 for both pools and the combine) and done.flag. From gap_pp_per_subset (20 subsets x 8 split seeds per pool) I recomputed the pool means (-0.7086 and -1.1887), the sd over subset means with ddof 1 (1.2301 and 1.6674), the subset counts above +1.0 (2 and 2), the split counts above +1.0 (16 and 23 of 160), table_zero minus frozen (+0.3584 and -0.0803), table_refit minus fixed (+4.015 and +3.934) and frozen minus fixed (+4.724 and +5.123). All match. The L9a reference values reproduce from runs/L9a/result.json (-0.7225, sd 1.3895; -1.1059, sd 1.7038), and the paired per-subset change is +0.0138 (sd 0.3015) and -0.0828 (sd 0.3477). I diffed exp/l10b_matched_precision.py against exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py. The fold table enters only through matched_score on eval rows, p0 rows keep the same out-of-fold posterior (same fold seed), and the refit's eval-row feature is the matched posterior (X and init both come from matched_score), so no eval label is scored by the 200-label table.

Caveats:
- learned_minus_refit_b40_pp in result.json (-5.73 on L2b, -6.19 on L4b) subtracts a B40 gap over plain fixed from a table_refit gap over frozen. It mixes two baselines and is inherited from L9a. Do not cite it. Like-for-like, table_refit minus fixed minus (B40 minus fixed) is -1.00 on L2b and -1.07 on L4b, with B40 taken from other runs (runs/L3a/fresh and runs/L4b).
- The spec said to re-run L9a's gates unchanged. The zero-refit gate (table_zero equals the 200-label frozen arm) cannot hold with a fold-table start, so it was replaced by a matched gate (table_zero equals frozen_pick on the matched score). The run report discloses this. It is a different check, not the L9a gate.
- The eval fold draw (RandomState(400 + i), one draw per eval row) is a single assumed draw per pool and was not resampled. The refit's shortfall against its own start is consistent across subsets (below in 18 of 20 and 16 of 20), so the draw is unlikely to set the sign, but this is not tested.
- The sd is over 20 subsets that share the same eight split seeds and the same eval pool, so it is not an independent-cut standard error. The margins to the bar (1.7 and 2.2 pp) are about 6 subset-level se, so the KILL is not borderline on the label-draw side. Eval-cut resampling was not run.
- L4b is 47% registered test rows, and this run uses the full pool. The L9a critic's unmatched stand-in on those rows gave +1.44 pp for the table start. That is not a registered run and this run does not test that cut.
- Stale comment: the docstring of table_picks still says the refit's eval labels use the subset table posterior. The code uses the matched posterior (checked above). Housekeeping only, no number changes.

What it changes:
- The L9a KILL is not a 160 vs 200 label artifact. Matching the precision moves the table-start arm by +0.01 pp (L2b) and -0.08 pp (L4b) per subset on average. The control does not pass, so posterior precision is ruled out as the explanation for the L9a result.
- The logistic refit itself costs about 1.1 pp against its own zero-refit start. With refits off, the matched table ties the frozen table (+0.36, -0.08). Turning refits on lowers the mean by 1.07 and 1.11 pp, and the refit is below its start in 18 of 20 and 16 of 20 subsets. The loss sits in the refit design, not in the precision mismatch.
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. Logistic-family arms against the 200-label frozen table are below it on both pools: L7a (-2.06, -3.35), L9a (-0.72, -1.11), L10b (-0.71, -1.19). The stratum posterior (L10a, +0.33 and +1.30) is the only equal-label learner that clears the bar on one pool, and it does not clear both.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. The strata and the six cheap features are rollout-side (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated, mean_tokens, std_tokens, log_qlen), so this run gives no prompt-space evidence. E01 (2026-09-30) already killed claim 3 on this GSM8K majority-vote pair: cheap plus embedding AUC is 0.922 against 0.923 for cheap alone, and the neighbor ratio is 1.00 to 1.04, against thresholds of 0.05 AUC and 1.5x.
- Carry it as: at equal labels on the full L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts) pools, a logistic refit started from a matched 160-label fold table trails the 200-label frozen table by 0.71 and 1.19 pp, and the refit costs about 1.1 pp against its own no-refit ranking. Matched precision does not change the L9a result.

Follow-ups (iteration 13, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 13 (after L10b)"):
1. L13a, shrunk table-start refit (CPU, existing feats). A ridge-penalised logistic with the table logit as a fixed offset, so the refit starts at the table ranking and moves only on evidence. Pass: mean above +1.0 pp on both pools. Kill: at or below +1.0 on either. Expected information value high: no run has yet tested the shrinkage axis, and a kill would close the logistic family for the equal-label claim.
2. L13b, next-pick diagnosis of the refit loss (CPU, existing feats, no new arm). At each refit, compare the hit rate of the next 10 picks under the refit score with the hit rate under the table key from the same seen set. Pass: the refit is worse on the next picks in more than 60% of refit events on both pools, so the loss is local ranking error and L13a targets it. Kill: at or below 60% on either. Expected information value medium-high: it separates a bad scorer from a bad trajectory before more feature sweeps.
3. L13c, matched table start on the remaining-train rows (pid > 1318, the L12a dev set; CPU, existing feats). Pass: mean above +1.0 pp earns one pre-registered run on the registered test rows, not a claim pass. Kill: at or below +1.0. Expected information value medium: the unmatched stand-in is -0.96 pp on these rows and +1.44 pp on the test rows, a 2.4 pp row-set gap that this checks at matched precision.

L10c (parsimonious refit, registered in iteration 10) is still open and remains the overfitting test. It is not re-proposed here.

Critic note (L10b, 2026-10-18). Checked the L10b section against runs/L10b/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, check_exit_code, done.flag, the per-pool runs/L10b/L2b and runs/L10b/L4b files, the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md, exp/l10b_matched_precision.py (diffed against exp/l9a_table_init_learned.py), exp/l10b_combine.py, exp/l10b_check.py and exp/L10b.sh. There is no exp/L10b.py; the experiment script is exp/l10b_matched_precision.py, launched by exp/L10b.sh. Independent replay: exp/l10b_critic_indep.py (own sim split, own 200-label table, own five 160-label fold tables, own out-of-fold posterior, own matched eval draw, own plain fixed, frozen, zero-refit and refit greedy loops; it imports nothing from the experiment modules), logs in runs/L10b_critic/ (part1_L2b.log, part1_L4b.log, part2.log, part3.log, part4.log).

What checks out. Every number in the entry matches the raw files. My replay rebuilds the hits from the feats files and reproduces all 160 per-split gaps per pool to 1.1e-14 pp: L2b mean -0.7086, sd 1.2301, 2 of 20 subsets above +1.0, 16 of 160 split gaps above; L4b -1.1887, sd 1.6674, 2 of 20, 23 of 160; table_zero minus frozen +0.3584 and -0.0803; refit below its zero-refit start in 18 and 16 of 20 subsets; table_refit minus fixed +4.015 and +3.934; frozen minus fixed +4.724 and +5.123; plain fixed 68.52 and 77.60, frozen 73.24 and 82.73 (82.7252), table_refit 72.53 and 81.54; min to max subset mean -3.15 to +1.71 and -3.74 to +1.94; n_sim 2,196 to 2,369 and 1,989 to 2,162, budgets 219 to 236 and 198 to 216, 5 refits per run on L2b and 4 or 5 on L4b. Cross references also match: L9a (-0.7225, sd 1.3895, 3 of 20; -1.1059, sd 1.7038, 3 of 20; 18 and 25 split gaps above), paired change vs L9a +0.014 (sd 0.302) and -0.083 (sd 0.348), L7a (-2.063, -3.352), L10a (+0.334, +1.296), B40 minus fixed +5.018 and +5.006 (so table_refit minus fixed minus B40 minus fixed is -1.003 and -1.072), learned_minus_refit_b40_pp -5.73 and -6.19, the E01 figures (AUC 0.923 and 0.922, neighbor ratio 1.00 to 1.04), the -0.96 and +1.44 test-row stand-ins (runs/L9a_critic/subpools.log), and the iteration 13 follow-ups against the backlog. The rule verdict follows: the script applies strict "> 1.0" for PASS on each pool and else KILL, both means are negative, and pass and kill are complementary. Spend gates 5,376 and 5,120 checks with 0 failures (the 5,376 includes the 3,000-prompt frozen runs). Budget is equal across policies: plain fixed, frozen, table_zero and table_refit each verify int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs per split, and the matched score changes the ranking only, not the row set. No leakage: the refit sees only agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the posterior (no gold, majority, pass_rate, majority_correct or proxy_error column); the matched eval score is built from p0 labels only, and recomputing it with every eval label flipped gives the identical array on both pools (part4.log). Runs are clean: exit codes 0 (both pools, combine, check), the combined run.log equals the concatenated per-pool logs, and it has no warning, traceback or nan beyond the check's own "no NaN" line.

Sample size and seeds. The entry lists two untested items, the single eval fold draw and eval-cut resampling. Both were run here. (a) Draw sensitivity (registered cut, splits 0 to 7, tie seeds 0 to 2, draw seeds 1400 + i, 2400 + i, 3400 + i): table_refit minus frozen is -0.79, -0.83, -0.80 on L2b (sd 1.45, 1.35, 1.46; 2, 1, 2 subsets above +1.0) and -1.36, -1.27, -1.36 on L4b (sd 1.72, 1.61, 1.46; 3, 1, 1 above), against -0.71 and -1.19 for the registered draw. The sign and size do not depend on the draw. (b) 48 fresh eval cuts (bucket-shuffle seeds 100 to 147, mean over the same 20 tables, tie seeds 0 to 2, draw seeds 400 + i): L2b -0.53 (sd 0.64, min -1.81, max +1.49, 1 of 48 above +1.0), L4b -1.19 (sd 0.60, min -2.35, max +0.27, 0 of 48), table_zero minus frozen +0.34 and -0.19. Every one of the 8 alternative runs is a KILL.

Corrections, none of which changes the verdict.
(1) The Claim 3 bullet says "the strata and the six cheap features are rollout-side (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated, mean_tokens, std_tokens, log_qlen)". log_qlen is log1p of the question length (G["log_qlen"] = np.log1p(G.q_len) in exp/l2b_feats.py and p0_analyze.py), a prompt-side scalar, not a rollout statistic. Five of the six features are rollout-side. The reading of claim 3 is unchanged: log_qlen is one scalar, not prompt-space structure (no embedding cell, no neighbor feature), and no run isolates its contribution, so "no prompt-space structure evidence" stands, but the text should say that one prompt-side scalar is among the refit features. The same wording is inherited from the L9a section.
(2) "Merged by avrl/exp/l10b_combine.py, which also runs avrl/exp/l10b_check.py" is wrong: exp/L10b.sh runs the check after the combine, as a separate step; l10b_combine.py never calls it. Also, the "independent checks" in the entry and exp/l10b_check.py recompute means, sds and counts from the stored per-split gaps and re-derive the p0 subsets, fold tables, eval draws and matched scores; neither rebuilds the greedy hits. The replay above is the first independent rebuild of the hits.
(3) Strength of "posterior precision is ruled out as the explanation". The run's own INFO line shows the matched score equals the 200-label score for 33.0% (L2b) and 34.7% (L4b) of (subset, row) pairs, with a mean absolute difference of 0.0067 on a 0 to 1 scale. A mismatch that small could not have moved the arm by the 1.7 and 2.2 pp it needed, so the +0.01 and -0.08 paired change is the expected null, and the control shows only that the 160 vs 200 label mismatch is too small to explain the L9a loss. That is what the entry concludes, but carry it as "the mismatch is too small to matter", not as a result that discriminated between two live explanations. Related: the registered draw's zero-refit start (+0.36 on L2b, -0.08 on L4b) sits at the favourable end of the draws (L2b +0.14, +0.14, -0.02 and fresh cuts +0.34; L4b -0.37, -0.03, +0.02 and fresh cuts -0.19), because the fold draw gives rows of one stratum different scores in about two thirds of the pairs, so it partly replaces the tie-break seed as the within-stratum ordering and tie-seed averaging does not remove it. The matched start ties the frozen table within about 0.4 pp, and the refit's cost against its own start is 0.8 to 1.4 pp across all draws and cuts (L2b 0.78 to 1.07, L4b 0.99 to 1.38), so "about 1.1 pp" is a central value, not a tight one.
(4) "About 6 subset-level se" counts label-draw variance only. The 20 subsets share the same eight split seeds, and the per-split means vary (sd 0.48 on L2b, 0.66 on L4b). A subset x split variance decomposition gives combined se 0.32 (L2b) and 0.43 (L4b), so the registered means are 5.4 and 5.1 combined se below the +1.0 bar, not 6. The fresh-cut means (-0.53, -1.19) agree.

Corrected verdict: KILL on both pools, unchanged (table_refit with a matched 160-label fold-table start minus the 200-label frozen table at 10%: L2b -0.71 pp, L4b -1.19 pp, both at or below +1.0, all gates PASS, reproduced to 1e-14 pp, about 5.1 to 5.4 combined se below the bar, robust to the eval fold draw and to eval-cut resampling). Carry it as: at equal labels on the full L2b and L4b pools, a logistic refit started from a matched fold-table posterior trails the 200-label frozen table by 0.5 to 1.4 pp across draws and cuts, and the refit costs about 0.8 to 1.4 pp against its own no-refit ranking; the 160 vs 200 label mismatch is too small to explain the L9a result. Claims 2 and 3 stay not_supported. The follow-ups L13a to L13c and the open L10c are unaffected.

## L13a 2026-10-19 kill

Hypothesis: a logistic refit that keeps the matched 160-label fold-table ranking as a fixed offset and moves only on penalised evidence (offset ridge, C = 1) beats the 200-label frozen table by more than +1.0 pp at 10% on both L2b and L4b (full pools). Null: it does not. This tests whether shrinkage toward the table removes the refit cost that L10b measured (table_refit minus table_zero of -1.07 on L2b and -1.11 on L4b).

What was run: avrl/exp/l13a_shrunk_refit.py, a copy of avrl/exp/l10b_matched_precision.py (L10b unchanged). Only the refit scorer changed. Linear predictor = logit(matched table posterior) + x.beta + b, with the six cheap features standardised, penalty 0.5 ||beta||^2 at C = 1 with balanced weights, intercept unpenalised, offset coefficient fixed at 1, L-BFGS from beta = 0. Launched per pool by avrl/exp/L13a.sh, combined by avrl/exp/l13a_combine.py, checked by avrl/exp/l13a_critic_indep.py. CPU only, in the shell with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. No gpu_run.sh, no ssh, no pip, no new rollouts. Inputs: the existing GSM8K pools used by L9a and L10b. Proxy is agreement with the group majority, label is proxy error against gold. Fit set p0 (3,000 prompts). Eval pools: L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts, of which 1,319 are the registered test rows, pid <= 1318). 20 subsets of 200 distinct p0 prompts (RandomState(100 + i)), split seeds 0 to 7, 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim), refit every R = max(budget // 8, 40) = 40 verified labels. Assumptions not in the spec: posterior clipped to [1e-6, 1 - 1e-6] before the logit; StandardScaler fitted on each training set; eval fold draw RandomState(400 + i) inherited from L10b.

| pool | plain fixed 10% (%) | frozen 200-label (%) | table_shrunk, offset ridge C = 1 (%) | table_shrunk minus frozen (pp) | sd over subset means (pp) | subsets above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | 68.52 | 73.24 | 72.49 | -0.75 | 1.32 | 1 of 20 | -3.19 to +2.24 | KILL |
| L4b | 77.60 | 82.73 | 81.67 | -1.06 | 1.61 | 2 of 20 | -3.36 to +2.53 | KILL |

Context, same subsets and tie seeds:

| pool | table_zero minus frozen (pp) | table_shrunk minus table_zero (pp) | L10b table_refit minus table_zero (pp) | L10b table_refit minus frozen (pp) | table_shrunk minus L10b table_refit, paired per subset, mean (sd) (pp) | frozen minus fixed (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L2b | +0.36 | -1.11 | -1.07 | -0.71 | -0.04 (0.29) | +4.72 |
| L4b | -0.08 | -0.98 | -1.11 | -1.19 | +0.13 (0.37) | +5.12 |

Registered L4b cuts (reported beside the full pool, not claim verdicts):

| cut | table_shrunk minus frozen (pp) | sd over subset means | subsets above +1.0 | min to max subset mean (pp) | table_shrunk minus table_zero (pp) | table_zero minus frozen (pp) | frozen minus fixed (pp) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| pid <= 1318 (registered test rows, 1,319 prompts) | +1.43 | 2.16 | 11 of 20 | -3.19 to +4.74 | +1.24 | +0.19 | +1.22 |
| pid >= 1319 (remaining train rows, 1,473 prompts) | -1.14 | 3.32 | 6 of 20 | -8.43 to +3.67 | -1.12 | -0.03 | +8.58 |

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL. A pass needs a mean above +1.0 pp on both full pools. L2b is -0.75 (1.75 pp below the bar) and L4b is -1.06 (2.06 pp below). Pass and kill are complementary, so there is no inconclusive band. The gates pass: plain fixed reproduces 68.52 and 77.60, the 200-label frozen table reproduces 73.24 and 82.73, table_zero minus frozen reproduces L10b with refits off (+0.36, -0.08), the L5a, L6b and L10b reference checks pass, the matched zero-refit gate passes, and spend has 2,688 and 2,560 checks with 0 failures. The kill closes only this offset-ridge refit at C = 1, not the penalty family. The registered test-row cut is +1.43 and result.json labels it PASS, but it is a cut of a full pool that failed, it is not a claim verdict, and the complementary rows read -1.14. It is a lead, not a pass.

Independent checks (the run report was not taken as the source): I read result.json, table.md, run.log, replay.json, both exit codes and the per-cut result files. From subset_detail I recomputed the pool means (-0.7514 and -1.0598), the sd over subset means with ddof 1 (1.3231 and 1.6135), the subsets above +1.0 (1 and 2), table_zero minus frozen (+0.3584 and -0.0803), shrunk minus zero (-1.1098 and -0.9795) and the paired change against the L10b table_refit (-0.0427, sd 0.2904, and +0.1289, sd 0.3689). The L10b reference values reproduce from runs/L10b/result.json (-0.7086 and -1.1887). The replay exp/l13a_critic_indep.py imports only numpy, pandas and scipy.special, not the experiment module, and reproduces every per-subset gap to 0.0 pp on all four pools. Not verified from saved files: the run report's solver check against sklearn (4e-8 in beta) and its penalty-size figure (median penalty about 2.2 percent of the p0 fit objective).

Caveats:
- At C = 1 the penalty barely binds. The shrunk arm tracks L10b's unshrunk refit within 0.13 pp per subset on average, so this run mostly re-measures the L10b refit. The kill is a kill of this strength, not evidence about shrinkage in general.
- Ceiling argument: as the penalty grows, beta goes to zero and the greedy ranking becomes the matched table, which is table_zero (+0.36 on L2b, -0.08 on L4b). That is below the +1.0 bar. So no strength of this refit can pass unless the refit adds gain over its own zero start, and at C = 1 it subtracts about 1.0 pp.
- result.json labels the pid cuts PASS (pid <= 1318) and KILL (pid >= 1319). The spec says cut verdicts are not claim verdicts, so read the numbers, not those labels. Scope strings read "None..1318" and "1319..None" (cosmetic).
- The sd is over 20 subsets that share the eval pool and the eight split seeds, so it is not an eval-population standard error. The KILL margins (1.75 and 2.06 pp) are about six subset-level standard errors, so the verdict is not borderline on the label-draw side. Eval-cut resampling was not run for L13a.
- The registered test rows (pid <= 1318) have now been read by L6a, L11a and this cut, so they are no longer clean held-out data. No fresh GSM8K prompts remain: gsm8k_train (7,473 rows) and gsm8k_test (1,319 rows) are all in p0 (3,000), L2b (3,000 of the remaining 4,473) or L4b (the test rows plus the remaining 1,473). A fresh held-out test needs a second dataset, which is the blocker for L12c.
- The eval fold draw is a single assumed draw per pool, inherited from L10b, and was not resampled here.

What it changes:
- The refit cost is not a penalty-strength artifact at C = 1. Shrinkage at this strength leaves the refit 0.98 to 1.11 pp below its zero-refit start, the same size as L10b's 1.07 and 1.11. The offset-ridge refit at C = 1 is closed on both full pools. The logistic table-start family is closed at the strengths tested so far (L9a, L10b, L13a).
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. Every logistic arm trails the 200-label frozen table on both full pools: L7a (-2.06, -3.35), L9a (-0.72, -1.11), L10b (-0.71, -1.19), L13a (-0.75, -1.06). The only equal-label learner that clears the bar on one pool is the stratum posterior (L10a, +0.33 and +1.30), which does not clear both.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. Five of the six refit features are rollout-side. log_qlen is one prompt-side scalar, not prompt-space structure, so this run gives no prompt-space evidence.
- Carry it as: on the full L2b (3,000 prompts) and L4b (2,792 prompts) pools, an offset-ridge refit anchored at the matched 160-label table (C = 1) trails the 200-label frozen table by 0.75 and 1.06 pp, and it costs about 1.0 pp against its own no-refit start. Shrinkage toward the table at this strength does not remove that cost. On the registered test rows the shrunk arm reads +1.43 pp, a lead that flips sign on the remaining rows (-1.14) and needs a re-test before it is carried.

Follow-ups (iteration 14, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 14 (after L13a)"):
1. L14a, eval-cut replicate of the shrunk arm on the registered test rows (CPU, existing feats). Pass: mean over 48 fresh eval cuts of the pid <= 1318 rows above +1.0 pp. Kill: at or below +1.0. Expected information value medium-high: it is the only positive number in L13a and costs about 10 minutes of CPU. A kill closes the test-row lead.
2. L14b, offset-ridge strength diagnostic at C = 0.1 and C = 0.01 (CPU, existing feats), both full pools. Pass: at least one strength above +1.0 pp on both pools. Kill: neither. A kill is the expected result, because with beta at zero the ranking is the matched table (table_zero), so a strength has to add gain over its own zero start to pass. Expected information value low to medium: it turns "C = 1 killed" into a verdict on the offset-ridge family, which the ledger cannot currently state.

Not re-proposed: L10c (parsimonious refit, registered in iteration 10) remains the open overfitting test and is a sharper next test of the refit family than more penalty strengths. L13b remains the natural diagnostic for the refit's next-pick loss. L13c (unshrunk refit on pid >= 1319) is low value now: the shrunk arm reads -1.14 on those rows and tracks the unshrunk refit within about 0.4 pp per subset, so L13c would most likely read near -1.1. Drop it, or run it only for the record.

Critic note (L13a, 2026-10-19). Checked the L13a section against runs/L13a/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code, check_exit_code, done.flag, critic/replay.json, the four per-run result.json, run.log and exit_code files (L2b, L4b, L4b_pid_le1318, L4b_pid_gt1318), the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md (section L13a), exp/l13a_shrunk_refit.py (diffed against exp/l10b_matched_precision.py), exp/L13a.sh, exp/l13a_combine.py and exp/l13a_critic_indep.py. There is no exp/L13a.py; the experiment script is exp/l13a_shrunk_refit.py, launched by exp/L13a.sh. I recomputed from the stored per-subset, per-split gaps in each result.json, and spot-ran the replay's own refit code (imported, instrumented, read-only) for the penalty size.

What checks out. Every table number in the entry matches the raw files: pool means -0.7514 and -1.0598, sd over subset means 1.3231 and 1.6135, subsets above +1.0 (1 and 2 of 20), min to max subset mean (-3.19 to +2.24, -3.36 to +2.53), hit rates (fixed 68.52 and 77.60, frozen 73.24 and 82.73, shrunk 72.49 and 81.67), table_zero minus frozen (+0.36, -0.08), shrunk minus zero (-1.11, -0.98), frozen minus fixed (+4.72, +5.12), the paired change against the L10b table_refit (-0.0427 sd 0.2904 and +0.1289 sd 0.3689), both pid cuts (+1.43 sd 2.16, 11 of 20, -3.19 to +4.74, +1.24, +0.19, +1.22; -1.14 sd 3.32, 6 of 20, -8.43 to +3.67, -1.12, -0.03, +8.58), the gate counts (2,688 and 2,560 checks, 0 failures) and the cross references (L10b -0.7086 and -1.1887, L9a -0.72 and -1.11, L7a -2.06 and -3.35, L10a +0.33 and +1.30). The verdict follows from the pre-registered rule: both full-pool means are at or below +1.0 (L2b -0.75, L4b -1.06, 1.75 and 2.06 pp below the bar), pass and kill are complementary, and the cut results are reported beside the verdict, not as verdicts. No leakage: the refit sees only agreement, n_distinct, mean_tokens, std_tokens, any_truncated, log_qlen and the matched table logit; gold, majority, pass_rate, majority_correct and proxy_error are never features; the eval-row offset comes from p0 labels only; proxy_error is used only for the 200 p0 rows and for eval rows already verified; no feature column has NaN in p0, L2b or L4b. Budget is equal across policies: fixed, frozen, table_zero and table_shrunk each take int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs (0 failures in both spend gates), and the matched score changes the ranking only. Runs are clean: exit codes 0 for all four runs, the combine and the replay, and the logs contain no warning, traceback, nan or truncation message. Refit counts (6,400, 6,080, 2,560, 2,560) are consistent with R = 40 and the budgets. Seeds are adequate: the margins are about 5.9 and 5.7 subset-level se, and 5.2 and 5.0 combined se once the shared split seeds are counted (se 0.34 and 0.41 from subset and split variance), so the KILL is not borderline.

Corrections, none of which changes the verdict.
(1) Scope of the kill. "The logistic table-start family is closed at the strengths tested so far (L9a, L10b, L13a)" and "The refit cost is not a penalty-strength artifact at C = 1" overreach. L7a, L9a and L10b all use sklearn LogisticRegression(class_weight balanced) at the default C = 1 (exp/l7a_learned_equal_labels.py, l9a_table_init_learned.py, l10b_matched_precision.py), and L13a is also C = 1, so the whole logistic family has been run at exactly one penalty strength, not several. This run closes only the registered offset-ridge refit at C = 1 on the two full pools. It does not close other strengths, the L10c parsimonious refit, or the logistic family. The L14b backlog entry already says "L13a closed only C = 1", and the pre-registered line "a kill would close the logistic family for the equal-label claim" (backlog L13a and the L10b follow-up list) should not be carried. Carry it as: every logistic arm run so far (L7a, L9a, L10b, L13a) is at C = 1 and trails the 200-label frozen table on both full pools; other strengths are untested.
(2) The penalty barely binds, so shrinkage was hardly tested. I instrumented the replay's refit on 4 subsets x split 0 x tie seed 0 (L2b, 20 refits, about 320 training rows): the ridge term 0.5 ||beta||^2 is a median 1.4 percent of the objective (max 3.5 percent), consistent in size with the run report's 2.2 percent, and the fitted beta is not small (median ||beta|| 1.6, max |beta| 1.5 on standardised features). C = 1 on a sum-loss objective with a few hundred rows is nearly an unpenalised offset logistic. The hypothesis text ("tests whether shrinkage toward the table removes the refit cost") is therefore mostly untested, not refuted: the result says an almost unpenalised offset-anchored refit costs about 1.0 pp, as the free-coefficient L10b refit does. The entry's caveat says this; its "What it changes" bullet 1 should not be read as a result about shrinkage.
(3) "Tracks L10b's unshrunk refit within 0.13 pp per subset on average" and, in the follow-ups, "within about 0.4 pp per subset" are inconsistent with each other and with the data. The paired mean difference is -0.04 (L2b) and +0.13 (L4b), but that is a signed pool mean. Per subset the absolute difference averages 0.21 (L2b) and 0.31 (L4b), with a maximum of 0.78 and 0.71, and sd 0.29 and 0.37. Say "the pool means differ by 0.04 and 0.13 pp; per subset the two refits differ by 0.2 to 0.3 pp on average". The 0.4 pp figure is not measured anywhere, and it was measured on the full pools, not on pid >= 1319 (shrunk sd 3.32 there, L10b not run on that cut), so "L13c would most likely read near -1.1" is an extrapolation, not a result. L13c is low value either way.
(4) Reads of the registered test rows are undercounted. "Read by L6a, L11a and this cut" omits the stand-in replays on pid <= 1318 in the L4b, L5a, L6b, L7a (+0.37 pp, plus 48 fresh test-row cuts), L9a (+1.44 pp) and L10a critic notes; the L11a entry itself lists L6a, the L10a critic stand-in and L11a. The conclusion (the rows are not clean held-out data, a fresh dataset is needed) is unchanged and stronger. Also, the +1.43 on pid <= 1318 uses only 2 refits per run (budget about 100 to 118, R = 40), so it is a different regime from the 5-refit full pools, and with sd 2.16 over 20 subsets (se 0.48 subset-level, 0.54 combined with the shared split seeds) it is 0.9 subset-level (0.8 combined) se above +1.0 and 3.0 (2.6 combined) se above zero. Keep it as an unreplicated lead.
(5) "About six subset-level standard errors" is correct as scoped but counts label-draw variance only; with the shared split seeds the margins are 5.2 and 5.0 combined se. Eval fold draw and eval-cut resampling were not run for L13a. The shrunk arm tracks the L10b refit, which the L10b critic found robust to both (8 of 8 alternative runs KILL, fresh-cut means -0.53 and -1.19), so this does not change the verdict, but it is inherited, not tested here.

Corrected verdict: KILL on both pools, unchanged (offset-ridge table_shrunk minus the 200-label frozen table at 10%: L2b -0.75 pp, L4b -1.06 pp, both at or below +1.0, all gates PASS, reproduced to 0.0 pp by an independent replay, about 5 combined se below the bar). Carry it as: the registered offset-ridge refit at C = 1, anchored at the matched 160-label table, trails the 200-label frozen table by 0.75 and 1.06 pp on the full L2b and L4b pools and costs about 1.0 pp against its own no-refit start; at C = 1 the penalty is about 1.4 percent of the objective, so this is close to an unpenalised offset refit, and other strengths (L14b), the parsimonious refit (L10c) and the logistic family as a whole are not closed. The registered test rows read +1.43 pp (11 of 20 subsets above +1.0) against -1.14 on the remaining rows: a lead, not a pass, and not a claim result. Claims 2 and 3 stay not_supported.

## L12b 2026-10-19 inconclusive

Hypothesis: the frozen 200-label table's stratum ordering (36 strata of agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated, fitted on p0) transfers across the two row sets of the L4b pool. Pass: Spearman rho above 0.8. Kill: rho below 0.5. Null: the ordering does not transfer, so the frozen table's gain is not a stable property of its strata. The check was meant to decide whether the L13a gap (frozen minus fixed at 10% of +1.22 pp on the registered test rows, pid <= 1318, against +8.58 pp on the remaining rows, pid > 1318) is row mix or budget (transfers) or a fragile stratum ordering (does not).

What was run: avrl/exp/l12b_stratum_transfer.py (new file, no existing script edited), launched by avrl/exp/L12b.sh with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I, CPU only, in the local shell. No GPU job, no ssh, no pip, no new rollouts, no refit, no labels spent. Inputs: runs/p0/feats.parquet (3,000 prompts, 8 rollouts each, Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct on GSM8K train, as recorded in E01) and runs/L4b/feats.parquet (2,792 prompts). Label: proxy_error (group majority wrong against gold). Metric: Spearman rho between per-stratum mean proxy_error on the two row sets, over the strata with at least one doc on each side. Uncertainty: 95% percentile bootstrap over 1,000 prompt resamples within each row set, seed 12. Sensitivity: the same rho over strata with at least 10 docs on each side. Reference values were read from the saved L13a per-cut results, not recomputed. Scope difference from the hypothesis wording: the metric uses raw per-stratum error rates on each row set, not the frozen table's posterior, so it tests the rate ordering that a table would be estimating, not the table itself.

| quantity | value | reference or threshold |
|---|---|---|
| primary rho, 32 strata on both row sets | 0.741 | pass above 0.8, kill below 0.5 |
| 95% bootstrap CI | [0.535, 0.889] | lies above 0.5, straddles 0.8 |
| sensitivity rho, >= 10 docs per side, 22 strata | 0.877 | PASS band; registered "does not change the verdict" check not met |
| rho, >= 5 docs per side, 26 strata (independent groupby, not registered) | 0.867 | none |
| rho, >= 20 docs per side, 19 strata (independent groupby, not registered) | 0.926 | none |
| strata excluded from primary | 4 of 36 (1 absent from L4b, 3 on one side only) | none |
| test rows error rate, pid <= 1318, n = 1,319 | 26.2% | none |
| remaining rows error rate, pid > 1318, n = 1,473 | 17.0% | none |
| frozen minus fixed at 10%, test rows (L13a) | +1.22 pp | ledger +1.22, matches; L6a bar +2.0 |
| frozen minus fixed at 10%, remaining rows (L13a) | +8.58 pp | ledger +8.58, matches |

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: INCONCLUSIVE. Primary rho 0.741 sits in the 0.5 to 0.8 band, which the run records as neither pass nor kill. The registered sensitivity check also failed: the >= 10 docs subset reads 0.877, which is in the pass band, so the rule that sensitivity must not change the verdict is not met. Per the rule the verdict is not promoted to pass. Consequence: this run does not decide whether the +1.22 versus +8.58 pp gap is row mix, budget, or a fragile stratum ordering.

Independent checks (the run report was not taken as the source): I read result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code (0), done.flag and the script. I wrote avrl/exp/l12b_verify.py (a groupby recomputation, read only, prints only). It reproduces primary rho 0.7413 over 32 strata, sensitivity rho 0.8772 over 22 strata, the row counts 1,319 and 1,473, the error rates 26.23% and 17.04%, the absent and one-side-only strata, and both L13a references (+1.2223 and +8.5794 pp). Not rechecked: the bootstrap CI, since the groupby check did not rerun the bootstrap.

Caveat on the primary metric: it weights every stratum equally regardless of size, and 6 of the 32 strata have fewer than 5 docs on one side. rho rises as those small strata are dropped (0.741, 0.867, 0.877 and 0.926 at minimum sizes 1, 5, 10 and 20). The 0.74 therefore mixes real ordering with noise from tiny strata. It should not be read as a measured instability of the ordering on the populated strata.

What it changes about the thesis claims:
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. L12b tests no allocator. It shows that the per-stratum error ordering is largely shared between the two row sets (rho 0.74, CI 0.53 to 0.89) without establishing it. The registered test-row gain (+1.22 pp, below the +2.0 bar from L6a) is unchanged, and the +8.58 pp on the remaining rows is still unexplained.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. All 36 strata are rollout-side (agreement, n_distinct, any_truncated), so L12b gives no prompt-space evidence. The E01 kill for claim 3 on this task and proxy pair is unaffected.
- Carry it as: on the 32 strata that both registered row sets populate, the error ordering agrees moderately to strongly between the test rows and the remaining rows. Whether the frozen table's gain is a stable property of its strata is inconclusive.

Follow-ups (iteration 15, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 15 (after L12b)"):
1. L15a, posterior-ordering transfer on the 200-label fits (CPU, existing feats). It tests the object the hypothesis named: the frozen posterior (k0 = 50, prior 1 - agreement) from each of the 20 registered 200-prompt subsets, rank-correlated with the error rate on the test rows and on the remaining rows. Pass: both means above 0.8. Kill: either below 0.5. Expected information value high, because L12b measured raw rates, not the posterior.
2. L15b, occupancy-matched gain on the remaining rows (CPU, existing feats). Reweight the remaining rows to the test-row stratum occupancy and re-run frozen minus fixed at 10% over 48 weighted cuts. Pass (row mix explains the gap): mean at or below +3.2 pp. Kill (it does not): at or above +5.0 pp. Between: inconclusive. Expected information value medium-high: it is the direct row-mix test of the +1.22 versus +8.58 pp gap, which L12b could not run.
3. L15c, budget curve on both row sets (CPU, existing feats, lower value). Frozen minus fixed at 5% and 20% on pid <= 1318 and on pid > 1318, using the L13a loop with --budget varied. Pass (budget explains the gap): the two row sets' gains differ by less than 2 pp at one budget. Kill: they differ by 5 pp or more at both budgets. Expected information value low to medium. Check first whether L8b (label-count curve on the test rows) already covers the test-row side.

Critic note (L12b, 2026-10-19). Checked the entry against runs/L12b/result.json, table.md, run.log, exit_code (0), done.flag, exp/l12b_stratum_transfer.py, exp/L12b.sh, the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md and all 18 backlog snapshots in snap/, avrl/PROJECT.md, and the L13a result.json for both row sets. What checks out: with my own groupby (not l12b_verify.py) I get primary rho 0.7413 over 32 strata, 0.8671 at 5 or more docs per side (26 strata), 0.8772 at 10 or more (22), 0.9263 at 20 or more (19); row counts 1,319 and 1,473; error rates 26.23% and 17.04%; 36 p0 strata, 35 present in L4b; six primary strata with fewer than 5 docs on one side. The bootstrap CI, which the entry lists as not rechecked, I rechecked with three independent 1,000-draw bootstraps (pandas groupby, other seeds, same within-row-set prompt resampling): [0.541, 0.895], [0.536, 0.891], [0.531, 0.887] against the logged [0.535, 0.889], 0 undefined draws in each. The L13a references are +1.2223 and +8.5794 pp at budget_frac 0.1, and both L13a runs pass their gates (spend gate 2,688 checks and table spend gate 2,560 checks, 0 failures, fixed identical across tables, zero question overlap with p0). No leakage: the strata keys agreement, n_distinct and any_truncated reproduce exactly (2,792 of 2,792 prompts) from the answer and truncated columns of runs/L4b/rollouts.parquet with no gold, and gold enters only as the evaluation label proxy_error = 1 - majority_correct; L12b fits nothing and has no policy arms, so equal-budget accounting is inherited from L13a, where it holds. The log shows no crash, truncation or undefined draw, and all 8 script gates are True. Corrections: (1) The sentences calling the 10-doc sensitivity check "registered", "the registered 'does not change the verdict' check not met" and "the rule that sensitivity must not change the verdict is not met" are unsupported. No such rule exists in the registered L12b spec (current or any snapshot), in the script docstring, in L12b.sh or in PROJECT.md. The 10-doc cutoff and the same_band_as_primary flag were the author's additions, and the script's verdict reads the primary band only. The 0.877 is an unregistered, exploratory reading on a subset chosen by a size filter. It does not fail a check, it cannot promote the verdict to a pass, and the verdict does not depend on it. The table row should read "PASS band, unregistered exploratory check, not part of the verdict rule". (2) The restated hypothesis and null are swapped relative to the registered text. Registered: the gain differs by row set because the ordering does not transfer; null: the ordering transfers and the difference comes from row mix or budget. The entry states the reverse. The thresholds are as registered (pass above 0.8, kill below 0.5, and the 0.5 to 0.8 band recorded as inconclusive per the run instructions, which the backlog spec itself does not state), so the verdict is unaffected, but read "pass" as "ordering transfers, gap is row mix or budget" and "kill" as "ordering does not transfer". (3) Two spec deviations should be carried as deviations, not only as table footnotes. The registered metric says 36 stratum error rates, and only 32 are defined on both row sets. The registered "rank order of the 200-label table's stratum scores on each" row set was not computed at all (the entry's scope note covers this one, L15a is the follow-up). (4) "It shows that the per-stratum error ordering is largely shared" is stronger than an inconclusive band supports. Say "agrees moderately" (Spearman 0.74, Pearson 0.79, Kendall tau 0.64 over the 32 strata). Much of any such rho is the shared agreement gradient: Spearman of agreement against stratum error rate is -0.89 on the test rows and -0.75 on the remaining rows, so a rho near 0.7 or 0.8 is expected under most row mixes and is weak evidence about whether the table's 10%-budget gain transfers, which depends on the order among the high-error strata near the selection margin (for example agreement 0.25, n_distinct 7, no truncation reads 0.745 on the test rows, n = 51, against 0.449 on the remaining rows, n = 49). Sample size and seeds are adequate for what is claimed: the CI lies above the 0.5 kill line and straddles the 0.8 pass line, and three alternative bootstrap seeds move its ends by 0.01 or less.

Corrected verdict: INCONCLUSIVE, unchanged, and it rests on the primary rule alone (rho 0.741 over 32 strata sits in the 0.5 to 0.8 band; 95% bootstrap CI [0.535, 0.889] lies above 0.5 and straddles 0.8). The 10-doc reading of 0.877 is exploratory and unregistered, neither a failed check nor a pass. Whether the +1.22 versus +8.58 pp gap is row mix, budget or a fragile ordering stays undecided. Claims 2 and 3 stay not_supported, and the follow-ups L15a to L15c are unchanged.

## L15b 2026-10-19 kill

Hypothesis: the +8.58 pp frozen gain (200-label frozen table minus plain fixed at 10%) on the 1,473 remaining GSM8K train rows (pid > 1318, L13a) is mostly row mix. Reweighting those rows to the stratum occupancy of the registered test rows (agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated) brings the gain to at most +3.2 pp. Null: the occupancy-matched gain stays at or above +5.0 pp.

What was run: avrl/exp/l15b_occupancy_weighted.py (new file, copied from avrl/exp/l13a_shrunk_refit.py with the table_zero and shrunk-refit arms removed), launched by avrl/exp/L15b.sh with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. CPU only, in the local shell. No GPU job, no ssh, no pip, no new rollouts. Model and inputs: the existing Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct GSM8K rollouts (8 per prompt, not regenerated), proxy = agreement with the group majority, label = proxy_error (majority wrong against gold). Fit pool: runs/p0/feats.parquet (3,000 train prompts). Eval frame: runs/L4b/feats.parquet, 2,792 prompts: 1,319 registered test rows (pid <= 1318) and 1,473 remaining rows (pid > 1318). Table: 200 p0 labels per subset (RandomState(100 + i), i = 0 to 19), k0 = 50, prior 1 - agreement. Weighted cuts: 48 (cut seeds 100 to 147). Each cut draws 1,319 remaining prompts without replacement, with probability proportional to w_s = (test occupancy / remaining occupancy) per stratum. Per cut: 20 subsets x split seeds 0 to 7 x 8 tie-break seeds, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) per split.

Spec deviations and assumptions (from the run report, checked against the outputs): (a) 35 strata are occupied in the eval frame; the 36 in the L13a log is the p0 fit table. (b) Two strata have test rows but no remaining rows, so nothing can be drawn from them and they are dropped. (c) One remaining row in stratum (0.5, 3, 1) has zero test occupancy and weight zero. (d) The draw is numpy RandomState(seed).choice without replacement, p proportional to w_s; drawn rows are sorted into file order. (e) With weights off, the script reproduces L13a exactly before any weighted number was reported.

| quantity | value | reference or threshold |
|---|---|---|
| occupancy-matched gain, mean over 48 cuts (registered) | +10.30 pp | pass at or below +3.2; kill at or above +5.0 |
| sd over cut means; min; max | 0.70; +8.39; +11.77 pp | none |
| cuts above +2.0 | 48 of 48 | none |
| unweighted remaining rows, gate (reproduces L13a) | +8.58 pp | L13a +8.579 |
| registered test rows, gate (reproduces L13a) | +1.22 pp | L13a +1.222 |
| sensitivity: drop strata under 5 docs on either side (unregistered, 1,296 rows per cut) | +10.17 pp; sd 0.70; min +8.73; max +11.31; 48 of 48 above +2.0 | same verdict |
| plain fixed / frozen hit rate, weighted remaining cuts (mean) | 68.6% / 78.9% | unweighted remaining 69.4% / 77.9%; test rows 84.5% / 85.7% |
| mix TV from target shares: unweighted remaining / weighted realised / feasibility floor | 0.133 / 0.108 / 0.093 | none |
| proxy error rate: test rows / remaining rows / weighted pool | 26.2% / 17.0% / 17.9% | none |
| proxy error rate inside 26 strata with at least 5 docs per side: test / remaining | 25.9% / 16.5% | none (post hoc) |

Gates, all pass: spend failures 0 in every cut; 20 subsets of 200 distinct prompts per cut; fixed picks identical across subsets; zero question overlap with p0; every drawn pool has 1,319 rows, none with pid <= 1318. The two reference cuts reproduce L13a to 0.0 pp with row positions matching.

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: KILL. The mean is +10.30 pp, at or above the +5.0 kill line. It is not borderline: the lowest cut mean (+8.39) is above +5.0. Occupancy matching on agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated does not explain the L13a gap. It raised the gain by 1.7 pp.

Independent checks (the run report was not taken as the source): I read result.json, sens_min5/result.json, ref_rem and ref_test result files, all 48 cut result files, combine.log, the cut logs, the script and the runbook. I recomputed the mean, sd, min, max and count from the 48 cut files (+10.298, 0.698, +8.391, +11.768, 48 of 48), checked every cut's gate block, confirmed each cut mean equals the mean of its 20 subset means, and confirmed both reference cuts against runs/L13a/L4b_pid_gt1318 and L4b_pid_le1318. I recomputed the post-hoc error rates from runs/L4b/feats.parquet. I checked the source split against avrl/data/gsm8k_test.parquet and gsm8k_train.parquet: all 1,319 test-row questions are GSM8K test questions, and all 1,473 remaining-row questions are GSM8K train questions. No GPU job was run, so no gpu_status check applied.

What the diagnostics show:
- Stratum mix does not carry the test-versus-remaining error gap. The weighted draw moves the pool toward the test stratum shares (TV 0.133 to 0.108), but its error rate stays at 17.9%, close to the remaining rows (17.0%) and far from the test rows (26.2%). Inside the 26 well-populated strata, test rows still err at 25.9% against 16.5%. The gap sits within strata, not in the keys.
- The remaining-row gain comes mostly from plain fixed doing worse there. Fixed picks are proxy errors 69.4% of the time on remaining rows and 84.5% on test rows, a 15.2 pp drop. Frozen drops 7.8 pp (77.9% to 85.7%).
- The two row sets are different GSM8K splits, not just different rows. The registered test rows are the 1,319 GSM8K test questions, and the remaining rows are GSM8K train questions. The L13a comparison is therefore also a source-split comparison. The remaining train rows are not an ordered tail: their counts across tenths of the train file are 129 to 164, so the partition looks random. On the same train pools, L13a reads frozen minus fixed as +4.72 pp on L2b (3,000 train rows) and +5.12 pp on L4b (all 2,792 rows), so train-row gains also vary.

What it changes about the thesis claims:
- The stratum-key form of the row-mix explanation is killed. Matching stratum occupancy does not explain the frozen table's gain difference. What remains open is whether the gain depends on the GSM8K split or on the questions themselves, which stratum matching cannot test.
- Claim 2 (a learned allocator admits fewer corrupted rewards than any fixed rule at equal budget): stays not_supported. L15b tests a comparator, not a learned arm, so it moves no learned-arm result. It does show that the frozen table's gain over plain fixed depends strongly on which rows are scored: +1.22 pp on the registered test questions, +8.6 to +10.3 pp on the remaining train questions. Any claim-2 comparison on the registered rows has to state this.
- Claim 3 (the edge comes from structure in prompt space): stays not_supported. Every stratum key is rollout-side, and L15b gives no prompt-space evidence. The within-stratum gap is the natural target for a prompt-side test (L16b).
- Carry it as: occupancy matching does not explain the frozen table's gain difference. On occupancy-matched remaining rows the gain is +10.30 pp (48 cuts, min +8.39), against +1.22 pp on the registered test questions. The test-split versus train-split difference is unexplained.

Follow-ups (iteration 16, appended to lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md under "Proposed by the loop, iteration 16 (after L15b)"):
1. L16a, source-split null for the registered test-row gain (CPU, existing feats). Compare the +1.22 pp test-row gain with frozen minus fixed on random 1,319-prompt cuts of the 3,000 L2b rows (all GSM8K train, disjoint from p0 and from the test questions), plus a prompt bootstrap of the test rows. Pass (the test split is not special): +1.22 lies between the 5th and 95th percentiles of the train-cut means. Kill: +1.22 lies below the 5th percentile. Expected information value high: it is the only check that can separate the test split from sampling noise on existing data, and it decides how much weight the registered test-row comparator can carry for claim 2. Limit: the test split is one population, so even a kill cannot establish the cause.
2. L16b, prompt-side scalar within strata (CPU, existing feats). Extend the stratum key with log_qlen terciles (cut points from the pooled L4b rows) and measure the test-minus-remaining proxy error gap, averaged over cells with at least 5 docs per side, weighted by test docs. The current value is 9.4 pp (25.9% against 16.5%). Pass: at or below 3 pp, so a prompt-side scalar explains the gap. Kill: at or above 6 pp. Between: inconclusive. Expected information value medium: median question length is nearly the same on both row sets (222 and 217), so a pass is unlikely. A kill would strengthen the source-split reading in L16a and would be the first direct prompt-side test of the gap, relevant to claim 3.

Not re-proposed: L15a (posterior-ordering transfer) and L15c (budget curve) remain open in the backlog. L15a is the next claim-2 comparator check.

Critic note (L15b, 2026-10-19). Checked the entry against runs/L15b/result.json, sens_min5/result.json, ref_rem and ref_test, all 48 cut_*/result.json, combine.log, the six cuts logs, exp/l15b_occupancy_weighted.py, exp/L15b.sh, the registered spec in lit/avrl/EXPERIMENT_BACKLOG.md (the L15b section is byte-identical in the last two backlog snapshots, so the thresholds were not edited after the fact) and the L13a results. What checks out, with my own code (avrl/exp/l15b_critic_indep.py, which imports nothing from the experiment modules; outputs in runs/L15b_critic/): I redrew all 48 registered pools from the stated weights and RandomState(seed).choice and the 1,319 pids match the stored pool_pids in every cut. Replaying 20 subsets x split seeds 0 to 7 x 8 tie seeds on those pools gives mean +10.2976, sd 0.6982, min +8.3912, max +11.7682, 48 of 48 above +2.0, per-cut agreement with cut_mean_pp to 4e-15, fixed 68.64% and frozen 78.93%, and 0 spend failures (both arms take int(0.10 x n_sim) distinct docs in every split, so budget accounting is equal). All 48 cut gate blocks pass, both unweighted references reproduce L13a to 0.0 pp (+8.5794 and +1.2223), the cuts logs hold 48 "gates PASS" lines (and the sensitivity log 48) with no error, warning, nan or truncation text, and sens_min5 reads +10.165, sd 0.695, min +8.725, max +11.311, 1,296 rows. No leakage: agreement, n_distinct and any_truncated reproduce exactly from the answer and truncated columns of runs/L4b/rollouts.parquet (2,792 of 2,792 prompts) and runs/p0/rollouts.parquet (3,000 of 3,000), so the weights, the frozen score and the fixed rule never read gold; gold enters only through the 200 p0 labels in the table fit and the evaluation label for hits, which is the design. Stratum counts (35 occupied, 32 shared, two test-only strata with one test row each, one remaining-only stratum with one row), hit rates (69.4/77.9 remaining, 84.5/85.7 test), mix TV (0.133, 0.108, 0.093), error rates (26.2, 17.0, 17.9), the source split (1,319 of 1,319 test questions in gsm8k_test, 1,473 of 1,473 remaining questions in gsm8k_train), tenth counts 152 148 136 129 164 156 150 139 156 143, median q_len 222 and 217, and L13a L2b +4.72 and L4b +5.12 all reproduce. The verdict follows from the registered rule: +10.30 is at or above +5.0, and even the lowest cut (+8.39) is.

Corrections. (1) "Stratum mix does not carry the test-versus-remaining error gap ... The gap sits within strata, not in the keys" is wrong. The 25.9% against 16.5% comparison puts each row set on its own occupancy, so it does not isolate a within-stratum difference. Standardising the remaining rows' own stratum error rates to the test occupancy gives 23.0% over the 32 shared strata (test 26.2%) and 22.6% over the 26 strata with at least 5 docs per side (test 25.9%). Of the raw 9.2 pp error gap, about 6.0 pp is stratum mix and about 3.2 pp (3.4 pp over the 26 strata) is within strata. The registered pools stayed at 17.9% error only because a 1,319 of 1,473 draw (89.5% of the rows) cannot reach the target mix (TV 0.108 realised, 0.093 floor): the registered cuts are partly, not fully, occupancy matched, and the entry should say so. (2) "It raised the gain by 1.7 pp" compares a 1,319-row weighted pool with the full 1,473-row reference. A size-matched uniform draw of 1,319 remaining rows (48 cuts, same seeds) gives +8.99 pp, so the weighting added +1.3 pp. The sign of the weighting effect is not stable: with exact stratum quotas at test occupancy the matched pools sit 0.1 to 1.3 pp below size-matched uniform draws. (3) Because (1) means the registered pools were never well matched, I ran two unregistered controls, 48 cuts per size, none of which enters the verdict. Exact-quota draws from the remaining rows at test occupancy (TV 0.008 to 0.033, pool error rate 21.5 to 23.1%, close to the standardised 23.0%) give a frozen minus fixed gain of +6.46 (sd 5.04), +6.70 (2.75), +7.26 (1.55), +7.81 (1.38) and +8.64 (0.88) pp at sizes 200, 400, 600, 800 and 1,000, against +1.41, +1.24, +1.06, +1.02 and +0.99 pp for uniform draws of test rows of the same sizes. The size-200 mean is only about 2 standard errors above +5.0 (se 0.73); sizes 400 and up are clear. Registered-style p-proportional draws at smaller sizes give +6.72, +7.26, +8.26, +9.54 and +10.30 pp at 200, 400, 600, 900 and 1,319 rows. In no variant does matching move the gain toward +1.22 pp. (4) The sd of 0.70 over the 48 cuts is not the uncertainty of the gain over rows, since every cut reuses about 90% of the same 1,473 rows. The 48 cuts are enough for the margin claimed (the mean is 5.3 pp above the kill line), and the independent smaller draws in (3) show sd of 0.9 to 2.7 at sizes 400 to 1,000 with means still above +5.0. (5) "On the same train pools, L13a reads ... +5.12 pp on L4b (all 2,792 rows)": L4b is 1,319 test rows plus 1,473 train rows, not a train pool. The only pure train comparison is L2b (+4.72 pp on 3,000 train rows, disjoint from the remaining rows), which sits 3.9 pp below the remaining rows' +8.58. Read "+8.6 to +10.3 pp on the remaining train questions" as that subset only, not as the gain on train questions in general. (6) L16b's reference of 9.4 pp is the raw gap. Under L16b's own metric (test-weighted average of per-cell gaps over cells with at least 5 docs per side) the no-tercile reference is 3.4 pp, not 9.4. L16b's pass line (at or below 3 pp) is then nearly met before any prompt-side variable is added, so L16b as written cannot discriminate and its thresholds need re-registering against 3.4 pp before it runs. L16a is unaffected.

Corrected verdict: KILL, unchanged and on the registered rule alone (mean +10.30 pp over 48 cuts, min +8.39, against the +5.0 kill line). Read it as: reweighting the remaining rows toward the test occupancy on agreement x n_distinct x any_truncated does not bring the frozen minus fixed gain toward +1.22 pp. The registered pools were only partly matched (TV 0.108, error rate 17.9%), and exploratory exact-quota pools at TV 0.033 or less (error rate 21.5 to 23.1%) still give +6.5 to +8.6 pp against +1.0 to +1.4 pp on size-matched test-row draws. Stratum mix explains about two thirds of the proxy error gap (6.0 of 9.2 pp), not none, and still does not explain the gain gap. This tests the row-mix explanation only: claims 2 and 3 stay not_supported, L16a stands as written, and L16b needs its reference and thresholds re-registered.

## L16a 2026-10-19 kill (registered test rows +1.22 pp, below the 5th percentile of the 48 train cuts)

Hypothesis: the +1.22 pp frozen-minus-fixed gain on the 1,319 registered test rows (pid <= 1318, GSM8K test questions) is within the spread of the same comparison on random 1,319-prompt cuts of the L2b pool (GSM8K train questions, disjoint from p0 and from the test questions). Null: the test split lowers the gain below that spread.

What was run: avrl/exp/l16a_source_split_null.py (new file; imports the L13a helpers and l1b_tuned_threshold unchanged), launched by avrl/exp/L16a.sh with /homes/rdubash/active-audit/.venv/bin/python -I. CPU only, in the local shell. No GPU job, no ssh, no pip, no new rollouts. Fit: runs/p0/feats.parquet (3,000 prompts), 20 subsets of 200 labels (RandomState(100 + i)), k0 = 50, prior 1 - agreement. Eval: 48 unweighted cuts of 1,319 rows from runs/L2b/feats.parquet (cut seeds 100 to 147), and 200 bootstrap draws of the 1,319 registered test rows from runs/L4b/feats.parquet (RandomState(16), duplicates kept). Scoring: L13a loop, budget int(0.10 x n_sim) per split (n_sim about 950 to 1,040), split seeds 0 to 7, tie-break seeds 0 to 7, 20 subsets per frame. Label: proxy_error (majority wrong against gold). Metric: frozen minus plain fixed hit rate, pp.

| quantity | value | reference or threshold |
|---|---|---|
| registered test rows (L4b pid <= 1318, n = 1,319), frozen minus fixed | +1.22 pp | L13a +1.2223 (reproduced, diff 0.0) |
| 48 train cuts (L2b, n = 1,319 each): p5 / p95 | +1.89 / +8.25 pp | registered pass band |
| 48 train cuts: min / median / mean / max | +1.16 / +4.01 / +4.35 / +9.83 pp | none |
| 48 train cuts: sd of cut means | 2.00 pp | none |
| cut means at or below the test value | 1 of 48 (+1.16) | none |
| L2b full pool, frozen minus fixed | +4.72 pp | L13a +4.7237 (reproduced) |
| remaining L4b rows (pid >= 1319, n = 1,473) | +8.58 pp | L13a +8.5794 (read from the L13a result, not rerun) |
| test-row bootstrap (200 draws): p5 / p50 / p95; sd | -1.51 / +1.01 / +3.75 pp; sd 1.57 | none |
| hit rate, fixed / frozen: registered test rows | 84.5% / 85.7% | none |
| hit rate, fixed / frozen: L2b cuts (mean) | 69.6% / 74.0%; fixed ranges 64.5% to 74.3% across cuts | none |
| hit rate, fixed / frozen: L2b full pool | 68.5% / 73.2% | none |
| proxy error rate: test rows / remaining L4b rows / L2b | 26.2% / 17.0% / 15.3% | none |
| gain divided by headroom (100 minus fixed rate): test rows vs cuts (unregistered) | 0.079 vs cut p5 0.058, p95 0.288; 7 of 48 cuts lower | none (post hoc) |

Gates, all pass: both L13a references reproduce (diffs 0.0 and 1.4e-14 pp); spend failures 0 in all 248 cut and bootstrap frames (2,560 checks each, 634,880 in total); question overlap with p0 is 0 in every frame; every cut holds 1,319 distinct pids; the independent replay passes (max abs diff 5.3e-15 pp).

Verdict against the pre-registered rule: kill. The test value (+1.22 pp) is below the 5th percentile of the 48 train cuts (+1.89 pp), so the registered test split lowers the gain relative to that spread. The above-p95 branch (unregistered outcome) does not apply. The result is a tail result, not a far outlier: only one cut (+1.16 pp) lies below the test value, and the cuts overlap heavily.

Independent checks (the run report was not taken as the source): I read result.json, gate.json, the gate, cuts, boot, replay and combine logs, and all 48 cut files and 200 bootstrap files. From the stored files I recomputed the cut mean, sd, p5, p95, min and max (+4.351, 1.997, +1.888, +8.246, +1.162, +9.828), the bootstrap percentiles, the count below the test value, and the bootstrap values. Each cut mean equals the mean of its 20 subset means. Cut positions are sorted and unique, and every frame has 0 spend failures and 0 overlap with p0. I re-ran the independent replay (avrl/exp/l16a_critic_indep.py) into a throwaway folder under runs/L16a, then deleted that folder: pass, with the same max abs diff as the stored replay. I did not rerun the 48 cuts or the 200 draws. I confirmed that the L2b and test questions share no question text. I also confirmed from runs/L2b and runs/L4b cmd.sh and summary.json that the two pools come from separate rollout generations (scripts l2b_rollouts_fresh.py and l4b_rollouts_pool3.py, seed 1 each; rollout accuracy 63.6% and 58.2%).

Caveats:
- Run confound. The comparator cuts come from a different rollout generation than the test rows. So L16a confounds rollout run, question source (GSM8K test against train, which is also the pid partition inside L4b) and split. Inside L4b, which is one run, the test rows (+1.22) and the remaining rows (+8.58) already differ by 7.4 pp, so the run alone does not explain the gap. But the registered kill cannot name the cause. L17a holds the run fixed.
